    "prompt_content": "{\n  \"attempt_id\": \"review-20260921T104030Z-763442\",\n  \"backend\": \"codex_exec\",\n  \"boundary_fingerprint\": null,\n  \"boundary_mode\": \"read-only-worker\",\n  \"capability_envelope_sha256\": \"28cb0f1d3d601800661d75b180c688ec47fe1f164ccfd593c22bae8fd3d2715f\",\n  \"goal_lineage\": {\n    \"binding\": null,\n    \"disposition\": \"not-goal-bound\",\n    \"draft\": null,\n    \"goal_run\": null,\n    \"kind\": \"charness.goal-lineage\",\n    \"reason\": \"critique review was run without a Goal Run Work Item identity\",\n    \"schema_version\": 1,\n    \"work_item\": null\n  },\n  \"kind\": \"charness.review_run_plan.v1\",\n  \"lens\": \"Assess the resolution and recurrence protection for each target, with an evidence-backed behavioral verdict. Pass only when the repair is supported; block or defer when it is not. Diagnosis alone is insufficient. Additional caller focus: Working tree is clean main HEAD plus uncommitted fix: contents are current evidence. Confirm: (1) the adapter contract orders the planner to report a declared specialized-lane command verbatim and never recompose argument forwarding, with the pnpm --source-sha worked example; (2) a regression test pins byte-identical passthrough of a pnpm-shaped lane command plus the contract wording. Pass only if both are present in these contents.\",\n  \"packet_path\": \"charness-artifacts/critique/review-20260921T104030Z-763442-packet.json\",\n  \"packet_sha256\": \"7958a9c5e4a203efc2fd6369a22c64cd6c6b1ad53c58e82cc37ac7d48e3c81a7\",\n  \"parent_receipt_identity\": \"parent-dbf3fca50503411c364e8ec2d854ddc945af4228c635ff13\",\n  \"reviewed_input_identity_sha256\": \"c0dece1f852a40b5b7feaf6190bb262f393af04252241ded1235d5bf45f07ec7\",\n  \"schema_sha256\": \"67288fc99eab9333366dfd0e07094b5b9dfec0f91f95b4e4cdc8a508a41c0304\",\n  \"scope\": \"issue-resolution: review resolution/recurrence and per-target behavioral observations for corca-ai/charness#817; do not merely diagnose causes\",\n  \"semantic_input\": {\n    \"entries\": [\n      {\n        \"carrier_path\": \"charness-artifacts/critique/workers/review-20260921T104030Z-763442/semantic-input/0000-content.bin\",\n        \"carrier_sha256\": \"7fef8577f9d1bd9ffd10de9b2f38b78553c1a32069a00346bc53284b420e45ca\",\n        \"content_sha256\": \"9cb2f32ad06da218d55a02332e7d0352dd8fcf47c52c44967cddc0525093da1a\",\n        \"disposition\": \"present\",\n        \"path\": \"skills/public/release/references/adapter-contract.md\",\n        \"prompt_content\": \"# Release Adapter Contract\\n\\n`release` stays portable by loading repo-specific version and install-surface\\nseams from a repo adapter.\\n\\n## Canonical Path\\n\\nUse `<repo-root>/.agents/release-adapter.yaml`.\\n\\n## Shared Core\\n\\n- `version`\\n- `repo`\\n- `language`\\n- `output_dir`\\n- `preset_id`\\n- `preset_version`\\n- `customized_from`\\n\\n## Release Fields\\n\\n- `package_id`\\n- `packaging_manifest_path`\\n- `materialized_plugin_root`\\n- `sync_command`\\n- `quality_command`\\n- `post_publish_install_refresh`\\n- `post_publish_version_readback`\\n- `post_publish_doctor_readback`\\n- `post_publish_distinct_channel_probe`\\n- `update_instructions`\\n- `requested_review_commands`\\n- `requested_review_policy`\\n- `review_unavailable_patterns`\\n- `review_waiver_phrases`\\n- `product_surfaces`\\n- `cli_skill_surface_probe_commands`\\n- `cli_skill_surface_command_docs`\\n- `cli_skill_surface_skill_paths`\\n- `cli_skill_surface_change_globs`\\n- `fresh_checkout_probes`\\n- `required_release_surfaces`\\n- `unpublished_release_surfaces`\\n- `require_derived_release_claims`\\n- `release_backend`\\n- `specialized_release_lanes`\\n\\n## Executed fields\\n\\n`sync_command` and `quality_command` are RUN by a subprocess; every other field in\\nthis contract is READ. That split is why their defaults below are the one place a\\nplaceholder would be wrong: a documentation placeholder in a read field is correct\\nand in an executed field is a broken command.\\n\\nBoth defaults name the AUTHORING repo's own tooling, so a consuming repo that never\\nwrote a release adapter inherits commands that cannot exist in its tree. Adapter\\nresolution warns when it can read the script path out of either value and that path\\nis missing, naming the field and whether the value was inferred or set. `bump_version`\\ngoes further and REFUSES on a missing `sync_command` target before it writes the\\nversion, because that command runs after the manifest is written and a failure there\\nwould leave a bumped manifest with an unsynced plugin mirror.\\n\\nThe reader is narrow on purpose: it resolves only `python3 <relative-path>` and\\n`./<relative-path>` where the path uses `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]` and nothing else. Every other\\nshape is silent — a pipeline or chain in any spelling, a redirect, an env prefix, a\\ndifferent interpreter, an interpreter option, a quoted, tilde, globbed or\\nbrace-expanded path. Silence means it did not judge, not that the command is fine. Set\\nboth fields to commands YOUR repo can run.\\n\\n## Quality scheduling\\n\\nExecute appends `--release-prepare` only to a simple native\\n`./scripts/run-quality.sh --release` invocation (optionally `--full`, `--read-only`,\\nor `--review`). Prepare runs all selected gates except `pytest-release`, replacing\\nthe last-release receipt with an **unestablished** result; storage failure fails\\npreparation. Resume runs the ordinary full release lane before publication.\\nPreparation cannot seal a final push receipt or satisfy the local commit floor.\\nCustom commands, pipelines and chains retain full execution at both stages.\\nDo not put `--release-prepare` in the adapter: execute owns that choice.\\n\\n## Defaults\\n\\n- `language`: `en`\\n- `output_dir`: `<repo-root>/charness-artifacts/release`\\n- `package_id`: repo directory name\\n- `packaging_manifest_path`: `<repo-root>/packaging/<repo>.json`\\n- `materialized_plugin_root`: `<repo-root>/plugins/<package_id>`\\n- `sync_command`: `python3 scripts/plugin_export/sync_root_plugin_manifests.py --repo-root .`\\n- `quality_command`: `<repo-root>/scripts/run-quality.sh`. Repos that gate\\n  release-time regression tests behind a pytest marker (e.g., `release_only`)\\n  should override this to include the marker — e.g.,\\n  `./scripts/run-quality.sh --release` — so publish covers update/install\\n  flow checks that standing pre-push intentionally skips.\\n  A repo may make one release's changed-line evidence an explicit non-claim with\\n  `./scripts/run-quality.sh --release --non-claim=release-changed-line-coverage`.\\n  This exact release-only label omits that lane rather than converting a failed\\n  verdict into success; all other release gates still run. Preserve the command\\n  in the tagged adapter and state the missing verdict in the release notes.\\n  When this repo-owned release command produces an established semantic receipt,\\n  the resume helper seals it to the exact clean HEAD/tree and materialized export.\\n  The child push may reuse that one-shot receipt for the pre-push broad lane; the\\n  close-keyword guard always runs, and any state drift falls back to the normal\\n  pre-push gate.\\n- `update_instructions`: empty list\\n- `requested_review_commands`: empty list\\n- `requested_review_policy`: `warn-if-unconfigured`\\n- `review_unavailable_patterns`: common release-record phrases such as\\n  `review unavailable`, `review gate unavailable`, and `executor_variants`\\n- `review_waiver_phrases`: `review waiver:`, `explicit review waiver:`, and\\n  `requested review waiver:`\\n- `specialized_release_lanes`: empty list. An optional list of mappings with\\n  `id`, `workflow`, `tag_pattern`, and `command` strings. A non-empty list is an\\n  explicit declaration that this repository's generic release route does not\\n  apply; the release planner reports the lane (or asks the operator to choose\\n  when several are declared) and does not execute its command. This is local\\n  declaration evidence only: hosted workflow state, tag triggers, and release\\n  approval remain outside this planner's claim. Report the declared `command`\\n  verbatim and never recompose its argument forwarding: the separator shape\\n  belongs to the target repo's declared script, not to the planner. Rewriting\\n  `pnpm run release:candidate:dispatch --source-sha <sha>` as\\n  `pnpm run release:candidate:dispatch -- --source-sha <sha>` hands the script\\n  a literal leading `--` it rejects (#817).\\n- `product_surfaces`: empty list\\n- `cli_skill_surface_*`: empty lists\\n- `fresh_checkout_probes`: empty list\\n- `required_release_surfaces`: empty list. Names the generated release surfaces the\\n  repo asserts it publishes, so an ABSENT one becomes drift instead of reading like a\\n  matching one. Known names: `claude_plugin`, `codex_plugin`,\\n  `claude_marketplace_version`, `codex_marketplace_source_path`. The packaging\\n  manifest is NOT declarable — its absence is drift whether or not anyone declares it.\\n- `unpublished_release_surfaces`: empty list. The OPT-OUT channel, and deliberately not\\n  an overload of the field above: because `required_release_surfaces` means \\\"these must\\n  exist\\\", naming a surface you do not publish there makes it drift, so it cannot be the\\n  remedy for not publishing it. Name here the generated surfaces this repo does not\\n  ship. Same known names.\\n- `require_derived_release_claims`: `true`. Notes handed to publish via\\n  `--notes-file` must carry a generated derived-claim block that agrees with the tree\\n  being shipped, and their authored prose must not carry an ungrounded quantity.\\n  Generate and check with\\n  `python3 \\\"$SKILL_DIR/scripts/generate_release_notes.py\\\" --repo-root . --notes-file <notes.md> --sync|--check`.\\n  **The default is `true` on purpose, and the direction is the point.** A gate armed by\\n  an opt-IN line is disarmed by deleting that line with nothing red; defaulting to true\\n  inverts it, so deleting the key — or the whole adapter — RE-ARMS the gate and the only\\n  way to publish unguarded notes is to write the opt-out down where a reviewer sees it.\\n  Setting it `false` disarms BOTH arms: the derived-block requirement and prose\\n  containment. Two things it does not do: it never runs on the resume lane (the window\\n  between the prepared stop and the resume is closed to worktree changes, so a refusal\\n  there would have no legal remedy), and it is never reached by a `--generate-notes`\\n  publish, which supplies no notes file for it to read.\\n\\n- **Absence is still never drift on its own.** A read-only `current_release` run never\\n  reddens a lane a consumer chose not to publish. What changed (D48) is that an absent\\n  surface named by NEITHER field makes `absence_corroboration` read `uncorroborated`,\\n  and `publish_release_preflight.release_surface_blocker` refuses the PUBLISH — the\\n  irreversible boundary — rather than letting a deleted declaration buy a silent green.\\n- A surface that is present but `unreadable` or missing its version IS drift without\\n  `required_release_surfaces`, because that is the state a failed sync leaves and a\\n  deleted declaration must not disarm it. It is still exemptable via\\n  `unpublished_release_surfaces`, and that matters more than it looks: the two\\n  marketplace surfaces are per-REPO files, not per-package, so a marketplace listing\\n  some other product parses fine, yields nothing for this package, and reads as\\n  `no-version` with nothing corrupt anywhere. `version` is likewise optional in an\\n  upstream plugin manifest.\\n- Honest residual: both fields are self-authored, and nothing checks that a declared\\n  surface is one the sync command actually produces. Deriving that from the sync channel\\n  was tried and withdrawn as unbuildable (the sync report names the plugin root as a directory, so two of the four surfaces never appear in it). The authoring repo's own D48 record of that is authoring-repo-internal and does not ship with this skill.\\n\\n## Artifact Rule\\n\\nThe durable release artifact filename is fixed:\\n\\n- `latest.md`\\n\\nDated release records should use `<repo-root>/charness-artifacts/release/YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>.md`.\\n\\n### Drafted release notes filename\\n\\nRelease notes drafted ahead of publish must carry `notes` as a whole\\n`-`/`_`-delimited token in the filename, alongside the version:\\n\\n- `<date>-<tag>-notes.md`\\n- `notes-<tag>.md` (the token may lead or trail)\\n- `<date>-<tag>-public-notes.md` (dash-separated version tokens are recognised)\\n\\nThis is the shape the pre-publish refusal looks for when it checks whether notes\\nwere drafted for the target tag and something else is being published. A draft\\nthat omits the token — `v1.2.3.md`, `<date>-<version>-release.md` — is INVISIBLE\\nto that arm, and publish will proceed with a generated body while the drafted\\nnotes sit unshipped. That is the v2.11.0 escape, and the convention is the part\\nof it a filename can fix.\\n\\nDeliberately narrow: the role word is matched as a whole token, not a substring,\\nand no other word is recognised. A wider match once made a dated\\n`<date>-<version>-release-record.md` read as drafted notes, and the refusal's\\nremedy asks the operator to rename or delete the file — advice that must never\\npoint at durable evidence.\\n\\n`release` should treat the packaging manifest as the canonical mutable version\\nsource. Generated plugin manifests and marketplace files are derived surfaces\\nand should be rewritten by the declared sync command, not edited by hand.\\n\\n`update_instructions` should name the canonical operator-facing refresh path for\\nalready published installs. Keep them evergreen: describe how to install the\\nlatest published release, not what changed in one release. Put release-specific\\nbehavior changes, migration notes, rollback advice, and rationale in release\\nnotes or the generated release artifact. Avoid host-internal compatibility detail\\nunless operators truly need it to complete the update.\\n\\n`requested_review_commands` is optional and exists for release workflows where\\nthe maintainer asks for a concrete review gate before publish. If any command\\nfails, `check_requested_review_gate.py` blocks publish/tag instead of treating\\nthe missing review surface as a caveat. The same helper scans release records\\nfor configured unavailable-review phrases; those records need a fix or an\\nexplicit review waiver phrase before release.\\n\\n`requested_review_policy` controls the empty-command posture. The default\\n`warn-if-unconfigured` keeps legacy repos noisy when no requested-review command\\nis wired. Repos that intentionally treat requested review as advisory declare\\n`advisory-only`; the checker then records `configuration_status: advisory_only`\\nwithout a warning. A configured command still blocks on failure under either\\npolicy.\\n\\n`fresh_checkout_probes` is optional command data owned by the repo adapter.\\nEach entry is a bash shell string executed from the temporary clone root. Use\\nit for release evidence that can pass in a maintainer worktree but fail from a\\nfresh checkout because of clone depth, generated artifact determinism, or other\\ncheckout-shape assumptions.\\n\\n`check_fresh_checkout_probes.py` reports `passed`/`blocked` (exit 0/1) only when\\nit actually ran the probes, which it does only under `--run-probes`. Declared but\\nnot run is `not_established` at exit 3 (`run-quality.sh`'s `UNESTABLISHED_EXIT`),\\ncarrying no `probe_results` key, because a listing is not a pass. A repo that\\ndeclares no probes is `not_configured` at exit 0 — a genuine opt-out that never\\nrefuses. `current_release.py` embeds the listing, so its fresh-checkout block is\\nalways `not_established` or `not_configured`, never a probe verdict.\\n\\n`publish_release.py --execute` runs declared probes in a temporary\\nshallow fresh clone after the release commit is created and before tag push or\\nrelease creation, records passing status in the release artifact, and reruns\\nthe probes against that amended release commit. A failing probe blocks publish.\\n\\nWhen `product_surfaces` contains both `installable_cli` and `bundled_skill`,\\nrelease runs the CLI plus bundled-skill disclosure gate only for matching CLI,\\nskill, plugin, package, or install-surface changes. Use\\n`cli_skill_surface_probe_commands` to point at binary-owned help, registry,\\ncatalog, example, version, install-smoke, doctor, or readiness probes. Keep\\nthese probes local and deterministic; latest-release, network, or upstream\\nfreshness checks belong in the release-specific proof that is intentionally\\nchecking freshness. Use\\n`cli_skill_surface_skill_paths` when the shipped skill does not live under\\n`skills/public/*` or `skills/support/*`.\\n\\n`release_backend` mirrors the `issue_backend` shape — with ONE measured exception, in the\\nposition that matters most for a template author: a `release_backend` command template\\n**includes the binary as its first element**, and `backend_command` never reads\\n`release_backend.binary`. (`issue_backend` templates exclude it and the issue backend prepends\\n`issue_backend.binary`.) That difference is why the two rules are not one function, and it is\\nexecuted rather than argued in\\n`<authoring-repo>/tests/quality_gates/test_release_backend_agrees_with_the_owner.py`.\\n\\nA template part containing a literal brace that is not a placeholder (a JSON payload, say)\\nis rendered through `str.format`, so double the braces (`{{\\\"q\\\":1}}`) or it fails fast.\\n\\nOtherwise `release_backend` mirrors `issue_backend` so release auth probes,\\nrelease-existence checks, and release-create calls can route through the\\nadapter-resolved CLI binary. Default is `{id: gh, binary: gh, commands: null}`,\\nwhich keeps the existing `gh release ...` shape. Hosts that resolve releases\\nthrough a different binary supply `commands` templates for `auth_check`,\\n`release_view` (uses `{tag}` substitution), `release_create` (uses `{tag}` and\\n`{title}` substitution), and optionally `release_view_body` (uses `{tag}`).\\nWithout commands, a non-`gh` backend errors at runtime instead of falling back\\nto `gh`.\\n\\n`release_view_body` reads the PUBLISHED release body back so the post-create\\nnotes audit can inspect it — the pre-publish audit only runs when a notes FILE\\nis supplied, so `--generate-notes` otherwise publishes a body nothing has seen.\\nIt is the one op a backend may omit safely: it runs after the release exists,\\nits result is advisory, and a missing template is recorded as\\n`published_notes_audit: not-configured` rather than raising. Declaring it means\\nthe audit actually inspects your published notes.\\n\\nThe placeholder set is enforced per op at runtime by\\n`publish_release_helpers.backend_command`: `release_view` accepts `{tag}`,\\n`release_create` accepts `{tag}` and `{title}`, and `auth_check` accepts no\\nplaceholders. An adapter template using an unknown placeholder fails fast with\\nthe offending placeholder named, matching the issue-backend close-with-comment\\npattern (`issue_close._resolve_op` per-op allowlist) so both backend surfaces\\nshare the same hardening shape. Adding a new placeholder requires updating\\n`OP_PLACEHOLDERS` in `publish_release_helpers.py` plus the call site and a\\nregression test.\\n\",\n        \"prompt_encoding\": \"utf-8\",\n run_loaded_script_main\\n\\nfrom .release_publish_fixtures import (\\n    PUBLISH_SCRIPT,\\n    REPO_ROOT,\\n    _seed_publish_release_repo,\\n)\\nfrom .seeding_support import load_module\\n\\nPLANNER = \\\"skills/public/release/scripts/plan_release_run.py\\\"\\npytestmark = pytest.mark.boundary_contract(\\n    reason=\\\"observe the release publish preparation's real git and external release child commands\\\"\\n)\\n\\n\\ndef _load_script_module(name: str, rel_path: str):\\n    return load_module(name, REPO_ROOT / rel_path)\\n\\n\\n_PLANNER = \\\"critique_blocked\\\": None,\\n        \\\"publish_current\\\": False,\\n        \\\"part\\\": None,\\n        \\\"set_version\\\": None,\\n        \\\"detail\\\": True,\\n    }\\n    values.update(overrides)\\n    return SimpleNamespace(**values)\\n\\n\\ndef _run_plan(\\n    repo: Path, env: dict[str, str], *args: str, detail: bool = True\\n) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:\\n    previous_cwd = Path.cwd()\\n    try:\\n        os.chdir(REPO_ROOT)\\n        cli_args = [\\\"--repo-root\\\", str(repo)]\\n        if detail:\\n            cli_args.append(\\\"--detail\\\")\\n        cli_args.extend(args)\\n        result = run_loaded_script_main(PLANNER, _PLANNER, *cli_args, env=env)\\n    finally:\\n        os.chdir(previous_cwd)\\n    return result\\n\\n\\ndef _run_plan_in_process(\\n    repo: Path, *args: str, check_current_status: bool = False\\n) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Run planner logic in-process for payload-only matrix cases.\\n\\n    These cases assert planner routing and packet shape, not the release status reader.\\n    Keep the dirty-tree case on the real status path, while clean payload cases avoid\\n    launching a second CLI and repeating a Git status read already covered by the release\\n    surface tests and the prepared-stop boundary test below.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    values = _args(repo_root=repo)\\n    iterator = iter(args)\\n    for value in iterator:\\n        if value == \\\"--part\\\":\\n            values.part = next(iterator)\\n        elif value == \\\"--publish-current\\\":\\n            values.publish_current = True\\n        elif value == \\\"--set-version\\\":\\n            values.set_version = next(iterator)\\n        elif value == \\\"--critique-blocked\\\":\\n            values.critique_blocked = next(iterator)\\n        elif value == \\\"--critique-artifact\\\":\\n            values.critique_artifact = next(iterator)\\n return_value=[])\\n    with (\\n        status_patch\\n        if not check_current_status\\n        else patch.object(\\n            _PLANNER._current_release, \\\"_git_status\\\", wraps=_PLANNER._current_release._git_status\\n        )\\n    ):\\n        payload = _PLANNER.build_plan(values)\\n    return subprocess.CompletedProcess(\\n        [\\\"plan_release_run.py\\\"], 0,     "path": "charness-artifacts/critique/workers/review-20260921T104030Z-763442/semantic-input/0000-content.bin",
    "prompt_content": "# Release Adapter Contract\n\n`release` stays portable by loading repo-specific version and install-surface\nseams from a repo adapter.\n\n## Canonical Path\n\nUse `<repo-root>/.agents/release-adapter.yaml`.\n\n## Shared Core\n\n- `version`\n- `repo`\n- `language`\n- `output_dir`\n- `preset_id`\n- `preset_version`\n- `customized_from`\n\n## Release Fields\n\n- `package_id`\n- `packaging_manifest_path`\n- `materialized_plugin_root`\n- `sync_command`\n- `quality_command`\n- `post_publish_install_refresh`\n- `post_publish_version_readback`\n- `post_publish_doctor_readback`\n- `post_publish_distinct_channel_probe`\n- `update_instructions`\n- `requested_review_commands`\n- `requested_review_policy`\n- `review_unavailable_patterns`\n- `review_waiver_phrases`\n- `product_surfaces`\n- `cli_skill_surface_probe_commands`\n- `cli_skill_surface_command_docs`\n- `cli_skill_surface_skill_paths`\n- `cli_skill_surface_change_globs`\n- `fresh_checkout_probes`\n- `required_release_surfaces`\n- `unpublished_release_surfaces`\n- `require_derived_release_claims`\n- `release_backend`\n- `specialized_release_lanes`\n\n## Executed fields\n\n`sync_command` and `quality_command` are RUN by a subprocess; every other field in\nthis contract is READ. That split is why their defaults below are the one place a\nplaceholder would be wrong: a documentation placeholder in a read field is correct\nand in an executed field is a broken command.\n\nBoth defaults name the AUTHORING repo's own tooling, so a consuming repo that never\nwrote a release adapter inherits commands that cannot exist in its tree. Adapter\nresolution warns when it can read the script path out of either value and that path\nis missing, naming the field and whether the value was inferred or set. `bump_version`\ngoes further and REFUSES on a missing `sync_command` target before it writes the\nversion, because that command runs after the manifest is written and a failure there\nwould leave a bumped manifest with an unsynced plugin mirror.\n\nThe reader is narrow on purpose: it resolves only `python3 <relative-path>` and\n`./<relative-path>` where the path uses `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]` and nothing else. Every other\nshape is silent — a pipeline or chain in any spelling, a redirect, an env prefix, a\ndifferent interpreter, an interpreter option, a quoted, tilde, globbed or\nbrace-expanded path. Silence means it did not judge, not that the command is fine. Set\nboth fields to commands YOUR repo can run.\n\n## Quality scheduling\n\nExecute appends `--release-prepare` only to a simple native\n`./scripts/run-quality.sh --release` invocation (optionally `--full`, `--read-only`,\nor `--review`). Prepare runs all selected gates except `pytest-release`, replacing\nthe last-release receipt with an **unestablished** result; storage failure fails\npreparation. Resume runs the ordinary full release lane before publication.\nPreparation cannot seal a final push receipt or satisfy the local commit floor.\nCustom commands, pipelines and chains retain full execution at both stages.\nDo not put `--release-prepare` in the adapter: execute owns that choice.\n\n## Defaults\n\n- `language`: `en`\n- `output_dir`: `<repo-root>/charness-artifacts/release`\n- `package_id`: repo directory name\n- `packaging_manifest_path`: `<repo-root>/packaging/<repo>.json`\n- `materialized_plugin_root`: `<repo-root>/plugins/<package_id>`\n- `sync_command`: `python3 scripts/plugin_export/sync_root_plugin_manifests.py --repo-root .`\n- `quality_command`: `<repo-root>/scripts/run-quality.sh`. Repos that gate\n  release-time regression tests behind a pytest marker (e.g., `release_only`)\n  should override this to include the marker — e.g.,\n  `./scripts/run-quality.sh --release` — so publish covers update/install\n  flow checks that standing pre-push intentionally skips.\n  A repo may make one release's changed-line evidence an explicit non-claim with\n  `./scripts/run-quality.sh --release --non-claim=release-changed-line-coverage`.\n  This exact release-only label omits that lane rather than converting a failed\n  verdict into success; all other release gates still run. Preserve the command\n  in the tagged adapter and state the missing verdict in the release notes.\n  When this repo-owned release command produces an established semantic receipt,\n  the resume helper seals it to the exact clean HEAD/tree and materialized export.\n  The child push may reuse that one-shot receipt for the pre-push broad lane; the\n  close-keyword guard always runs, and any state drift falls back to the normal\n  pre-push gate.\n- `update_instructions`: empty list\n- `requested_review_commands`: empty list\n- `requested_review_policy`: `warn-if-unconfigured`\n- `review_unavailable_patterns`: common release-record phrases such as\n  `review unavailable`, `review gate unavailable`, and `executor_variants`\n- `review_waiver_phrases`: `review waiver:`, `explicit review waiver:`, and\n  `requested review waiver:`\n- `specialized_release_lanes`: empty list. An optional list of mappings with\n  `id`, `workflow`, `tag_pattern`, and `command` strings. A non-empty list is an\n  explicit declaration that this repository's generic release route does not\n  apply; the release planner reports the lane (or asks the operator to choose\n  when several are declared) and does not execute its command. This is local\n  declaration evidence only: hosted workflow state, tag triggers, and release\n  approval remain outside this planner's claim. Report the declared `command`\n  verbatim and never recompose its argument forwarding: the separator shape\n  belongs to the target repo's declared script, not to the planner. Rewriting\n  `pnpm run release:candidate:dispatch --source-sha <sha>` as\n  `pnpm run release:candidate:dispatch -- --source-sha <sha>` hands the script\n  a literal leading `--` it rejects (#817).\n- `product_surfaces`: empty list\n- `cli_skill_surface_*`: empty lists\n- `fresh_checkout_probes`: empty list\n- `required_release_surfaces`: empty list. Names the generated release surfaces the\n  repo asserts it publishes, so an ABSENT one becomes drift instead of reading like a\n  matching one. Known names: `claude_plugin`, `codex_plugin`,\n  `claude_marketplace_version`, `codex_marketplace_source_path`. The packaging\n  manifest is NOT declarable — its absence is drift whether or not anyone declares it.\n- `unpublished_release_surfaces`: empty list. The OPT-OUT channel, and deliberately not\n  an overload of the field above: because `required_release_surfaces` means \"these must\n  exist\", naming a surface you do not publish there makes it drift, so it cannot be the\n  remedy for not publishing it. Name here the generated surfaces this repo does not\n  ship. Same known names.\n- `require_derived_release_claims`: `true`. Notes handed to publish via\n  `--notes-file` must carry a generated derived-claim block that agrees with the tree\n  being shipped, and their authored prose must not carry an ungrounded quantity.\n  Generate and check with\n  `python3 \"$SKILL_DIR/scripts/generate_release_notes.py\" --repo-root . --notes-file <notes.md> --sync|--check`.\n  **The default is `true` on purpose, and the direction is the point.** A gate armed by\n  an opt-IN line is disarmed by deleting that line with nothing red; defaulting to true\n  inverts it, so deleting the key — or the whole adapter — RE-ARMS the gate and the only\n  way to publish unguarded notes is to write the opt-out down where a reviewer sees it.\n  Setting it `false` disarms BOTH arms: the derived-block requirement and prose\n  containment. Two things it does not do: it never runs on the resume lane (the window\n  between the prepared stop and the resume is closed to worktree changes, so a refusal\n  there would have no legal remedy), and it is never reached by a `--generate-notes`\n  publish, which supplies no notes file for it to read.\n\n- **Absence is still never drift on its own.** A read-only `current_release` run never\n  reddens a lane a consumer chose not to publish. What changed (D48) is that an absent\n  surface named by NEITHER field makes `absence_corroboration` read `uncorroborated`,\n  and `publish_release_preflight.release_surface_blocker` refuses the PUBLISH — the\n  irreversible boundary — rather than letting a deleted declaration buy a silent green.\n- A surface that is present but `unreadable` or missing its version IS drift without\n  `required_release_surfaces`, because that is the state a failed sync leaves and a\n  deleted declaration must not disarm it. It is still exemptable via\n  `unpublished_release_surfaces`, and that matters more than it looks: the two\n  marketplace surfaces are per-REPO files, not per-package, so a marketplace listing\n  some other product parses fine, yields nothing for this package, and reads as\n  `no-version` with nothing corrupt anywhere. `version` is likewise optional in an\n  upstream plugin manifest.\n- Honest residual: both fields are self-authored, and nothing checks that a declared\n  surface is one the sync command actually produces. Deriving that from the sync channel\n  was tried and withdrawn as unbuildable (the sync report names the plugin root as a directory, so two of the four surfaces never appear in it). The authoring repo's own D48 record of that is authoring-repo-internal and does not ship with this skill.\n\n## Artifact Rule\n\nThe durable release artifact filename is fixed:\n\n- `latest.md`\n\nDated release records should use `<repo-root>/charness-artifacts/release/YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>.md`.\n\n### Drafted release notes filename\n\nRelease notes drafted ahead of publish must carry `notes` as a whole\n`-`/`_`-delimited token in the filename, alongside the version:\n\n- `<date>-<tag>-notes.md`\n- `notes-<tag>.md` (the token may lead or trail)\n- `<date>-<tag>-public-notes.md` (dash-separated version tokens are recognised)\n\nThis is the shape the pre-publish refusal looks for when it checks whether notes\nwere drafted for the target tag and something else is being published. A draft\nthat omits the token — `v1.2.3.md`, `<date>-<version>-release.md` — is INVISIBLE\nto that arm, and publish will proceed with a generated body while the drafted\nnotes sit unshipped. That is the v2.11.0 escape, and the convention is the part\nof it a filename can fix.\n\nDeliberately narrow: the role word is matched as a whole token, not a substring,\nand no other word is recognised. A wider match once made a dated\n`<date>-<version>-release-record.md` read as drafted notes, and the refusal's\nremedy asks the operator to rename or delete the file — advice that must never\npoint at durable evidence.\n\n`release` should treat the packaging manifest as the canonical mutable version\nsource. Generated plugin manifests and marketplace files are derived surfaces\nand should be rewritten by the declared sync command, not edited by hand.\n\n`update_instructions` should name the canonical operator-facing refresh path for\nalready published installs. Keep them evergreen: describe how to install the\nlatest published release, not what changed in one release. Put release-specific\nbehavior changes, migration notes, rollback advice, and rationale in release\nnotes or the generated release artifact. Avoid host-internal compatibility detail\nunless operators truly need it to complete the update.\n\n`requested_review_commands` is optional and exists for release workflows where\nthe maintainer asks for a concrete review gate before publish. If any command\nfails, `check_requested_review_gate.py` blocks publish/tag instead of treating\nthe missing review surface as a caveat. The same helper scans release records\nfor configured unavailable-review phrases; those records need a fix or an\nexplicit review waiver phrase before release.\n\n`requested_review_policy` controls the empty-command posture. The default\n`warn-if-unconfigured` keeps legacy repos noisy when no requested-review command\nis wired. Repos that intentionally treat requested review as advisory declare\n`advisory-only`; the checker then records `configuration_status: advisory_only`\nwithout a warning. A configured command still blocks on failure under either\npolicy.\n\n`fresh_checkout_probes` is optional command data owned by the repo adapter.\nEach entry is a bash shell string executed from the temporary clone root. Use\nit for release evidence that can pass in a maintainer worktree but fail from a\nfresh checkout because of clone depth, generated artifact determinism, or other\ncheckout-shape assumptions.\n\n`check_fresh_checkout_probes.py` reports `passed`/`blocked` (exit 0/1) only when\nit actually ran the probes, which it does only under `--run-probes`. Declared but\nnot run is `not_established` at exit 3 (`run-quality.sh`'s `UNESTABLISHED_EXIT`),\ncarrying no `probe_results` key, because a listing is not a pass. A repo that\ndeclares no probes is `not_configured` at exit 0 — a genuine opt-out that never\nrefuses. `current_release.py` embeds the listing, so its fresh-checkout block is\nalways `not_established` or `not_configured`, never a probe verdict.\n\n`publish_release.py --execute` runs declared probes in a temporary\nshallow fresh clone after the release commit is created and before tag push or\nrelease creation, records passing status in the release artifact, and reruns\nthe probes against that amended release commit. A failing probe blocks publish.\n\nWhen `product_surfaces` contains both `installable_cli` and `bundled_skill`,\nrelease runs the CLI plus bundled-skill disclosure gate only for matching CLI,\nskill, plugin, package, or install-surface changes. Use\n`cli_skill_surface_probe_commands` to point at binary-owned help, registry,\ncatalog, example, version, install-smoke, doctor, or readiness probes. Keep\nthese probes local and deterministic; latest-release, network, or upstream\nfreshness checks belong in the release-specific proof that is intentionally\nchecking freshness. Use\n`cli_skill_surface_skill_paths` when the shipped skill does not live under\n`skills/public/*` or `skills/support/*`.\n\n`release_backend` mirrors the `issue_backend` shape — with ONE measured exception, in the\nposition that matters most for a template author: a `release_backend` command template\n**includes the binary as its first element**, and `backend_command` never reads\n`release_backend.binary`. (`issue_backend` templates exclude it and the issue backend prepends\n`issue_backend.binary`.) That difference is why the two rules are not one function, and it is\nexecuted rather than argued in\n`<authoring-repo>/tests/quality_gates/test_release_backend_agrees_with_the_owner.py`.\n\nA template part containing a literal brace that is not a placeholder (a JSON payload, say)\nis rendered through `str.format`, so double the braces (`{{\"q\":1}}`) or it fails fast.\n\nOtherwise `release_backend` mirrors `issue_backend` so release auth probes,\nrelease-existence checks, and release-create calls can route through the\nadapter-resolved CLI binary. Default is `{id: gh, binary: gh, commands: null}`,\nwhich keeps the existing `gh release ...` shape. Hosts that resolve releases\nthrough a different binary supply `commands` templates for `auth_check`,\n`release_view` (uses `{tag}` substitution), `release_create` (uses `{tag}` and\n`{title}` substitution), and optionally `release_view_body` (uses `{tag}`).\nWithout commands, a non-`gh` backend errors at runtime instead of falling back\nto `gh`.\n\n`release_view_body` reads the PUBLISHED release body back so the post-create\nnotes audit can inspect it — the pre-publish audit only runs when a notes FILE\nis supplied, so `--generate-notes` otherwise publishes a body nothing has seen.\nIt is the one op a backend may omit safely: it runs after the release exists,\nits result is advisory, and a missing template is recorded as\n`published_notes_audit: not-configured` rather than raising. Declaring it means\nthe audit actually inspects your published notes.\n\nThe placeholder set is enforced per op at runtime by\n`publish_release_helpers.backend_command`: `release_view` accepts `{tag}`,\n`release_create` accepts `{tag}` and `{title}`, and `auth_check` accepts no\nplaceholders. An adapter template using an unknown placeholder fails fast with\nthe offending placeholder named, matching the issue-backend close-with-comment\npattern (`issue_close._resolve_op` per-op allowlist) so both backend surfaces\nshare the same hardening shape. Adding a new placeholder requires updating\n`OP_PLACEHOLDERS` in `publish_release_helpers.py` plus the call site and a\nregression test.\n",

    "prompt_content": "from __future__ import annotations\n\nimport os\nimport re\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom types import SimpleNamespace\nfrom unittest.mock import patch\n\nimport pytest\nimport yaml\n\nfrom tests.script_main import run_loaded_script_main\n\nfrom .release_publish_fixtures import (\n    PUBLISH_SCRIPT,\n    REPO_ROOT,\n    _seed_publish_release_repo,\n)\nfrom .seeding_support import load_module\n\nPLANNER = \"skills/public/release/scripts/plan_release_run.py\"\npytestmark = pytest.mark.boundary_contract(\n    reason=\"observe the release publish preparation's real git and external release child commands\"\n)\n\n\ndef _load_script_module(name: str, rel_path: str):\n    return load_module(name, REPO_ROOT / rel_path)\n\n\n_PLANNER = _load_script_module(\"plan_release_run_test_module\", PLANNER)\n_BUMP = _load_script_module(\n    \"bump_version_test_module\",\n    \"skills/public/release/scripts/bump_version.py\",\n)\n_PACKETS = _load_script_module(\n    \"plan_release_run_packets_test_module\",\n    \"skills/public/release/scripts/plan_release_run_packets.py\",\n)\n_PREPARED_STOP = _load_script_module(\n    \"plan_release_prepared_stop_test_module\",\n    \"skills/public/release/scripts/plan_release_prepared_stop.py\",\n)\n_CLOSEOUT_TOKENS = _PLANNER.release_binding_tokens\n_CLOSEOUT_EVIDENCE = _PLANNER._closeout_evidence\n\n\ndef _args(**overrides: object) -> SimpleNamespace:\n    values = {\n        \"repo_root\": REPO_ROOT,\n        \"remote\": \"origin\",\n        \"critique_artifact\": None,\n        \"critique_blocked\": None,\n        \"publish_current\": False,\n        \"part\": None,\n        \"set_version\": None,\n        \"detail\": True,\n    }\n    values.update(overrides)\n    return SimpleNamespace(**values)\n\n\ndef _run_plan(\n    repo: Path, env: dict[str, str], *args: str, detail: bool = True\n) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:\n    previous_cwd = Path.cwd()\n    try:\n        os.chdir(REPO_ROOT)\n        cli_args = [\"--repo-root\", str(repo)]\n        if detail:\n            cli_args.append(\"--detail\")\n        cli_args.extend(args)\n        result = run_loaded_script_main(PLANNER, _PLANNER, *cli_args, env=env)\n    finally:\n        os.chdir(previous_cwd)\n    return result\n\n\ndef _run_plan_in_process(\n    repo: Path, *args: str, check_current_status: bool = False\n) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:\n    \"\"\"Run planner logic in-process for payload-only matrix cases.\n\n    These cases assert planner routing and packet shape, not the release status reader.\n    Keep the dirty-tree case on the real status path, while clean payload cases avoid\n    launching a second CLI and repeating a Git status read already covered by the release\n    surface tests and the prepared-stop boundary test below.\n    \"\"\"\n    values = _args(repo_root=repo)\n    iterator = iter(args)\n    for value in iterator:\n        if value == \"--part\":\n            values.part = next(iterator)\n        elif value == \"--publish-current\":\n            values.publish_current = True\n        elif value == \"--set-version\":\n            values.set_version = next(iterator)\n     "carrier_path": "charness-artifacts/critique/workers/v893-release-critique-a/semantic-input/0042-content.bin",

    "disposition": "present",
    "path": "docs/implementation-discipline.md",
    "prompt_content": "# Implementation Discipline\n\n> Status: current\n> Source of truth: this page and the executable commands it names\n> Last verified: 2026-09-02\n\nThis page answers one question: how do we make a change, learn whether it\nworked, and leave the next change easy to start? It is intentionally short.\n\n## Default loop\n\n1. Read [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md), [docs/index.md](./index.md), and the owner page for the surface being\n   changed. For an active Goal Run, read the parent issue and the one child\n   selected by its cursor; read the full graph only for explicit sync or parent\n   close.\n2. Inspect the exact target and current diff. Keep the parent worktree intact.\n   Implementation and proof worktrees must be clean at creation and use a\n   separate named branch. Runtime caches and temporary output live outside the\n   worktree.\n3. Make the smallest change that improves the user's path. Removing obsolete\n   code, wrappers, gates, mirrors, docs, or tests is a valid implementation\n   when their consumers have been checked. Do not add a rule to compensate for\n   a problem that deletion or derivation removes.\n4. Run focused tests for the changed behavior, commit the slice, run the\n   changed-line proof over `base..HEAD`, and only then the default core lane\n   when the changed surface has cross-module consumers; the order and its cost\n   are owned by [parallel execution](./parallel-execution.md#disjoint-writers).\n   `./scripts/run-quality.sh --release` is the release-final changed-line\n   coverage and mutation proof; it is not part of ordinary implementation.\n   A slow or conditional gate is not silently part of ordinary implementation.\n5. If a source surface exports a generated mirror, run its canonical exporter\n   once after batching source edits, then validate the result. The source is\n   authoritative; mirror drift is a release/package concern, not a reason to\n   duplicate authoring work.\n6. What is pushed is the verified commit: a repair that verification forced\n   lands as a further commit on the same unpushed branch, never as a silent\n   edit after the proof. The external phases that need an explicit request\n   are listed in [operating contract](./operating-contract.md#external-changes).\n\nWhen a documentation change is unusually broad, [authoring-preflight](./authoring-preflight.md)\nand [check_doc_authoring_preflight.py](../scripts/gates/check_doc_authoring_preflight.py)\nare authoring affordances that forecast the relevant checks. When deleting a\nwrapper or symbol in `docs/` or `skills/`, [check_symbol_residue.py](../scripts/gates/check_symbol_residue.py)\nremains advisory by design (#259): use it to find consumers, then let the owning\nfocused test decide.\n\n## Repair posture\n\nEvery failure is repaired structurally, not naively fixed and retried. Ask\nwhether the repair changes the pattern that produced the failure, and the\npattern behind that pattern. The [north star](./design-north-star.md) stays\nthe governing standard; this section is where its repair half is worked.\n\nTreat these as defects rather than preferences, and repair the pattern that\nproduced each one:\n\n- a hardcoded value with no stated basis;\n- a fact copied instead of derived from the source of truth that owns it;\n- coupling or validation beyond what the boundary needs;\n- a behavior bent to satisfy a failing validator instead of first suspecting\n  the validator;\n- two gates with one intent, or two bootstrap paths for one job;\n- code or tests that exist for a moment rather than for a behavior, coupled to\n  a date, an incident, a run id, or a count;\n- a test whose result depends on wall-clock time or machine environment;\n- a blurred domain-model boundary;\n- backward compatibility carried during development for something never\n  shipped;\n- worse resource efficiency in tokens, disk, memory, or processor time,\n  whether for this repository or for the people running what it builds.\n\nBeyond this list, look creatively for anything that makes the code and the\nenvironment better. There is always a better way.\n\n## Worktree and runtime hygiene\n\nWorktree rules are owned by\n[operating contract](./operating-contract.md#git-and-worktrees) and the commands\nby [worktree prepare](./worktree-prepare.md).\n\n## Proof proportionality\n\nProof proportionality is owned by\n[operating contract](./operating-contract.md#verification); the `prove` skill is\nan evidence formatter for the cases it lists, not a universal stop ceremony.\n\n## Shared commands\n\nThe quality lanes, the standing runner, and the\n[check-docs.sh](../scripts/check-docs.sh) receipt are in\n[development](./development.md#verification-and-export); the\ndirect-`pytest` export line is in\n[operating contract](./operating-contract.md#generated-surfaces).\n",

sys.path:\n        sys.path.insert(0, str(root))\n\n\n_load_repo_runtime_bootstrap()\n\nfrom scripts.runtime_bootstrap import import_repo_module  # noqa: E402\nfrom scripts.task_run import payload[\"phase\"] = phase\n    payload[\"timestamps\"][stamp] = _support.utc_now_iso()\n    return time.monotonic()\n\n\ndef _record_timing(payload: dict[str, Any], key: str, origin: float) -> None:\n    payload[\"timings_ms\"][key] = int((time.monotonic() - origin) * 1000)\n\n\ndef _persist(payload: dict[str, Any], runtime_path: Path) -> None:\n    stamps = payload.setdefault(\"timestamps\", {})\n    stamps[\"updated_at\"] = _support.utc_now_iso()\n    if payload.get(\"phase\") == \"terminal\":\n        stamps[\"finished_at\"] = stamps[\"updated_at\"]\n    _support.write_task_result(runtime_path, payload)\n\n\ndef _persist_completion(payload: dict[str, Any], runtime_path: Path) -> None:\n    candidate = payload.get(\"candidate\")\n    persist_status = (\n        candidate.get(\"persist\", {}).get(\"status\") if isinstance(candidate, dict) else None\n    )\n    if (\n        payload.get(\"status\") == \"completed\"\n        and isinstance(candidate, dict)\n        and candidate.get(\"status\") == \"validated\"\n        and persist_status != \"committed\"\n        and not candidate.get(\"head_is_complete\", True)\n    ):\n        if candidate.get(\"carrier_kind\") == \"worktree-only\":\n            payload[\"next_step\"] = (\n                f\"Review the complete validated candidate in {payload['worktree_path']}; \"\n                \"no lane commit exists, so lane HEAD is not the complete candidate.\"\n            )\n        else:\n            payload[\"next_step\"] = (\n                f\"Review the complete validated candidate in {payload['worktree_path']}; \"\n                \"the lane HEAD commit is a proper subset of the complete candidate. \"\n                \"Carry the committed_paths and dirty_paths before treating it as integrated.\"\n            )\n    _persist(payload, runtime_path)\n\n\ndef _terminal(\n    payload: dict[str, Any],\n    runtime_path: Path,\n    *,\n    status: str,\n    next_step: payload\n\n\ndef _complete_task(\n    payload: dict[str, Any],\n    *,\n    runtime_path: Path,\n    resolved_target: Path,\n    resolved_repo: Path,\n    before_exec: dict[str, list[str]],\n scope_specs=scope_specs,\n        require_change=require_change,\n        parent_before=parent_before,\n        parent_before_head=parent_before_head,\n        stdout_log=stdout_log,\n    persist=_persist_completion,\n        result_delivery=_support._result_delivery,\n        completion_evidence=_completion_evidence,\n        execution_state=_execution_state,\n Path | None = None,\n    branch: str | None = None,\n    base: str | None = None,\n    lane: str | None = None,\n    scopes: Sequence[str],\n    prompt: str,\n    codex: str = \"codex\",\n    executor: str | None = None,\n    effort: str | None = None,\n    task_id: str | None = None,\n    prepare: bool | None = None,\n    require_change: bool | None = None,\n    skip_prepare: bool =     repo_root=resolved_repo or repo_root.expanduser().resolve(),\n            target_path=(resolved_target or target_path or Path.cwd()).expanduser().resolve(),\n            task_id=task_id,\n            error=str(exc),\n        )\n\n    resolved_lane = resolved[\"lane\"]\n    resolved_target = resolved[\"target_path\"]\n    resolved_branch = resolved[\"branch\"]\n    resolved_base = resolved[\"base\"]\n    base_sha = resolved[\"base_sha\"]\n    normalized_scopes = resolved[\"scopes\"]\n    codex_path = resolved[\"codex_path\"]\n    resolved_executor = resolved[\"executor\"]\n \"task-run\",\n        \"status\": FAIL,\n        \"phase\": \"planned\" if dry_run else \"running\",\n        \"approval_eligibility\": \"ineligible\",\n        \"dry_run\": dry_run,\n        \"task_id\": resolved_task_id,\n        \"repo_root\": str(resolved_repo),\n        \"worktree_path\": str(resolved_target),\n        \"branch\": resolved_branch,\n        \"base\": resolved_base,\n effort=resolved[\"effort\"],\n        timeout_seconds=timeout_seconds,\n    )\n    if resolved_lane is not None:\n        payload[\"lane\"] = resolved_lane\n    if dry_run:\n        payload[\"status\"] = PASS\n        payload[\"approval_eligibility\"] = \"not-applicable\"\n        payload[\"next_step\"] = (\n            \"Re-run without --dry-run to create the named worktree and execute \"\n            f\"{resolved_executor}.\"\n        )\n        payload[\"actions\"] = [\n            {\"id\": \"create-worktree\", \"status\": \"planned\"},\n            {\n                \"id\": f\"{resolved_executor}-exec\",\n                \"status\": \"planned\",\n                \"cwd\": str(resolved_target),\n            },\n        ]\n        return payload\n\n    payload[\"status\"] = \"running\"\n    started_at = _mark_phase(payload, \"create\", \"create_started_at\")\n    _persist(payload, runtime_path)\n    try:\n        resolved_target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        create_payload = _worktree.run_create(\n            resolved_repo,\n            target_path=resolved_target,\n            branch=resolved_branch,\n            base=base_sha,\n            prepare=resolved_prepare,\n            # Lanes of one repo share one installed-dependency cache under the\n            # repo's runtime root, not the lane's private execution root (#792).\n            dependency_cache_root=runtime_path / _worktree._doctor_lib.DEPENDENCY_CACHE_DIR_NAME,\n        )\n    except KeyboardInterrupt:\n        return _terminal(\n            payload,\n            runtime_path,\n            status=\"interrupted\",\n            next_step=\"Task creation was interrupted; inspect the target path before retrying _support.record_create(payload, create_payload)\n    _record_timing(payload, \"create\", started_at)\n    if not create_payload.get(\"created\") or (\n        resolved_prepare and executor=resolved_executor,\n            executable=codex_path,\n            effort=resolved[\"effort\"],\n            worktree=resolved_target,\n        )\n        payload[\"git_worktree_dir\"] = payload[\"before_exec\"] = _snapshot_payload(before_exec)\n        preflight_populations = _population_delta({}, before_exec, preflight=True)\n        payload[\"preflight_populations\"] = preflight_populations\n        if any(preflight_populations[name][\"verdict\"] == FAIL for name in (\"tracked\", \"untracked\")):\n            return _terminal(\n                payload,\n                runtime_path,\n                status=\"failed\",\n                next_step=f\"The newly-created worktree was not clean before {resolved_executor}; inspect it and use a fresh path.\",\n            )\n\n        log_dir = runtime_path / \"task-run\" / resolved_task_id\n        log_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        stdout_log = log_dir / f\"{resolved_executor}.stdout.log\"\n        stderr_log = log_dir / f\"{resolved_executor}.stderr.log\"\n        payload[\"logs\"] = {\"stdout\": str(stdout_log), \"stderr\": str(stderr_log)}\n        exec_started_at = _mark_phase(payload, \"exec\", \"exec_started_at\")\n        _persist(payload, runtime_path)\n\n        print(\n            f\"task run: executing {resolved_executor} in {resolved_target}\",\n            file=sys.stderr,\n        )\n        execution = _execute_codex(\n            command,\n            prompt=lane_prompt,\n            target_path=resolved_target,\n            configured_env=configured_env,\n            stdout_log=stdout_log,\n            stderr_log=stderr_log,\n            timeout_seconds=timeout_seconds,\n        )\n        _record_timing(payload, \"exec\", exec_started_at)\n        candidate_commit = None\n        abnormal = _abnormal_exit_state(execution)\n        if abnormal is not None:\n            try:\n                candidate_commit = _lane_runner._checkpoint_interrupted_lane(\n                    resolved_target, base_sha, scope_specs\n                )\n            except (\n                OSError,\n                RuntimeError,\n                TypeError,\n                TaskRunError,\n                subprocess.SubprocessError,\n            ) as exc:\n                payload[\"execution\"] = {**execution, \"status\": abnormal}\n                payload[\"candidate\"] = {\n                    \"status\": \"wip\",\n                    \"useful\": False,\n                    \"changed_paths\": [],\n                    \"state\": \"interrupted-mid-edit\",\n                    \"state_known\": False,\n                    \"commit\": {\n                        \"status\": \"failed\",\n                        \"error\": str(exc),\n                        \"correctness_verified\": False,\n                    },\n                }\n                return _terminal(\n                    payload,\n                    runtime_path,\n                    status=abnormal,\n                    error=f\"{abnormal} WIP candidate commit failed: {exc}\",\n                    next_step=(\n                        f\"The {abnormal} WIP checkpoint could not be committed; inspect and \"\n                        \"recover the retained worktree manually.\"\n                    ),\n                )\n        return _complete_task(\n            payload,\n            runtime_path=runtime_path,\n            resolved_target=resolved_target,\n            resolved_repo=resolved_repo,\n            before_exec=before_exec,\n            base_sha=base_sha,\n            scope_specs=scope_specs,\n            require_change=resolved_require_change,\n            parent_before=parent_before,\n            parent_before_head=parent_before_head,\n            stdout_log=stdout_log,\n            execution=execution,\n            started_at=started_at,\n            candidate_commit=candidate_commit,\n            target_head=(\n                str(candidate_commit[\"sha\"])\n                if candidate_commit is not None and candidate_commit.get(\"status\") == \"committed\"\n                else None\n            ),\n        )\n    except KeyboardInterrupt:\n        return _terminal(\n            payload,\n            runtime_path,\n            status=\"interrupted\",\n            next_step=\"Task lifecycle was interrupted; inspect the retained worktree before retrying.\",\n        )\n    except (OSError, RuntimeError, TypeError, TaskRunError, subprocess.SubprocessError) as exc:\n        return _terminal(\n            payload,\n            runtime_path,\n            status=\"failed\",\n            error=f\"task lifecycle failed: {exc}\",\n            next_step=\"Inspect the retained worktree and lifecycle error, then retry with a fresh lane.\",\n        )\n\n\ndef task_status(repo_root: Path, task_id: str | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Read the one external task-run result store without mutation.\"\"\"\n    resolved_repo = _support._require_git_root(repo_root)\n    runtime_path = _support.task_runtime_root(resolved_repo)\n    if task_id is not None:\n        record = _support.read_task_result(runtime_path, task_id)\n        if record is not None:\n            return {**record, \"liveness\": _support.runner_liveness(record)}\n        return {\n            \"schema_version\": _support.SCHEMA_VERSION,\n            \"event\": \"task-status\",\n            \"repo_root\": str(resolved_repo),\n            \"task_id\": task_id,\n            \"status\": \"missing\",\n            \"result_path\": str(_support.task_result_path(runtime_path, task_id)),\n        }\n    return {\n        \"schema_version\": _support.SCHEMA_VERSION,\n        \"event\": \"task-status-list\",\n        \"repo_root\": str(resolved_repo),\n        \"runtime_root\": str(runtime_path),\n        \"tasks\": [\n            {**record, \"liveness\": _support.runner_liveness(record)}\n            for record in _support.read_task_results(runtime_path)\n        ],\n    }\n",

    "path": "scripts/task_run/task_run_completion_next_step.py",
    "prompt_content": "\"\"\"Render operator-facing next steps for completed task-run candidates.\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Mapping\n\n\ndef _candidate_location(\n    payload: Mapping[str, object], resolved_target: Path, candidate: Mapping[str, object]\n) -> str:\n    if candidate.get(\"head_is_complete\") and payload.get(\"target_branch\") and payload.get(\"target_sha\"):\n        return f\"on branch {payload['target_branch']} at {payload['target_sha']}\"\n    return f\"in {resolved_target}\"\n\n\ndef _next_step(\n    payload: Mapping[str, object],\n    *,\n    resolved_target: Path,\n    candidate: Mapping[str, object],\n    execution_status: str,\n    result_state: str,\n    blockers: list[str],\n) -> str:\n    location = _candidate_location(payload, resolved_target, candidate)\n    if execution_status == \"timed-out\":\n        suffix = f\"; {'; '.join(blockers)}.\" if blockers else \".\"\n        if (candidate.get(\"commit\") or {}).get(\"status\") != \"committed\":\n            return (\n                f\"No scoped changes were retained from the timed-out lane — no WIP \"\n                f\"candidate commit was created; inspect the retained worktree in \"\n                f\"{resolved_target}\"\n                + suffix\n            )\n        return (\n            f\"Review the committed WIP candidate in {resolved_target}; \"\n            \"interrupted mid-edit — state unknown; the commit is not a correctness claim\"\n            + suffix\n        )\n    if blockers:\n        return (\n            f\"Inspect the retained candidate {location}, typed result, and captured logs; \"\n            + \"; \".join(blockers)\n            + \".\"\n        )\n    if result_state == \"validated-partial-result\":\n        return (\n            f\"Review the validated candidate {location}; \"\n            \"it is useful but not approval-eligible.\"\n        )\n    return f\"Review the candidate {location}; the typed result is approval-eligible.\"\n",
