    "prompt_content": "\"\"\"Git and linked-worktree state for bounded task runs.\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport hashlib\nimport os\nimport re\nimport stat\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\nfrom collections.abc import Callable\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any, Mapping, Sequence\n\n\ndef _load_repo_runtime_bootstrap():\n    pathlib, sys = __import__(\"pathlib\"), __import__(\"sys\")\n    marker = (\"scripts\", \"adapter_lib.py\")\n    parents = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parents\n    root = next((p for p in parents if p.joinpath(*marker).is_file()), None)\n    if root is not None and str(root) not in sys.path:\n        sys.path.insert(0, str(root))\n\n\n_load_repo_runtime_bootstrap()\n\nfrom scripts.core.git_checkout import head_oid_from_files as _head_sha_from_checkout  # noqa: E402\nfrom scripts.core.git_checkout import (  # noqa: E402\n    identity_from_files,\n    layout_from_files,\n    worktree_root_from_files,\n)\nfrom scripts.core.git_status_snapshot import GitStatusError  # noqa: E402\nfrom scripts.worktree.checkout_view import (  # noqa: E402\n    CheckoutView,\n    GitCheckout,\n    moment_from_status,\n)\n\ntry:\n    from scripts.core.subprocess_guard import run_process\nexcept ImportError:  # flat layout: the script dir is on sys.path, the repo root is not\n    _scripts_dir = next(\n        ancestor / \"scripts\"\n        for ancestor in Path(__file__).resolve().parents\n        if (ancestor / \"scripts\" / \"core\" / \"subprocess_guard.py\").is_file()\n    )\n    if str(_scripts_dir) not in sys.path:\n        sys.path.insert(0, str(_scripts_dir))\n    from subprocess_guard import run_process\nfrom scripts.task_run.task_run_contract import (  # noqa: E402\n    _BRANCH_RE,\n    _GIT_DISCOVERY_ENV,\n    FAIL,\n    PASS,\n    TaskRunError,\n)\n\nWIP_CANDIDATE_COMMIT_MESSAGE = \"task-run: WIP candidate — interrupted mid-edit — state unknown\"\nPERSIST_CANDIDATE_COMMIT_MESSAGE = \"task-run: persist validated candidate onto the lane branch\"\n\n\ndef _git_env() -> dict[str, str]:\n    return {key: value for key, value in os.environ.items() if key not in _GIT_DISCOVERY_ENV}\n\n\ndef _git(repo_root: Path, *args: str) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:\n    return run_process([\"git\", *args], cwd=repo_root, env=_git_env(), timeout_seconds=None)\n\n\ndef _git_output(repo_root: Path, *args: str) -> str:\n    if args == (\"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\"):\n        oid = _head_sha_from_checkout(repo_root)\n        if oid:\n            return oid\n    result = _git(repo_root, *args)\n    if result.returncode != 0:\n        detail = result.stderr.strip() or result.stdout.strip() or \"git command failed\"\n        raise TaskRunError(f\"git {' '.join(args)} failed: {detail}\")\n    return result.stdout\n\n\ndef _repo_snapshot(repo_root: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Read immutable repository identity and topology from checkout files.\n\n    The task lifecycle needs the same four scalar facts at its input boundary:\n    the worktree root, the shared administration directory, the checkout's\n    administration directory, and ``HEAD``.  Ordinary layouts already state\n    them; ``rev-parse`` remains the fallback for redirected or packed layouts.\n    \"\"\"\n    requested = repo_root.expanduser().resolve()\n    identity = identity_from_files(requested)\n    if identity is not None:\n        if identity.repo_root != requested:\n            raise TaskRunError(\n                f\"--repo-root must be the Git worktree root, not a subdirectory: {requested}\"\n            )\n        return {\n            \"repo_root\": identity.repo_root,\n            \"git_common_dir\": identity.common_dir,\n            \"git_dir\": identity.git_dir,\n            \"head\": identity.head_oid,\n        }\n    values = _git_output(\n        requested,\n        \"rev-parse\",\n        \"--show-toplevel\",\n        \"--git-common-dir\",\n        \"--git-dir\",\n        \"HEAD\",\n    ).splitlines()\n    if len(values) != 4:\n        raise TaskRunError(\"git rev-parse returned an incomplete repository snapshot\")\n    discovered = Path(values[0]).resolve()\n    common = Path(values[1])\n    if not common.is_absolute():\n        common = discovered / common\n    git_dir = Path(values[2])\n    if not git_dir.is_absolute():\n        git_dir = discovered / git_dir\n    common = common.resolve()\n    git_dir = git_dir.resolve()\n    if discovered != requested:\n        raise TaskRunError(\n            f\"--repo-root must be the Git worktree root, not a subdirectory: {requested}\"\n        )\n    if not common.is_dir():\n        raise TaskRunError(f\"Git common directory is not a directory: {common}\")\n    if not git_dir.is_dir():\n        raise TaskRunError(f\"Git directory is not a directory: {git_dir}\")\n    return {\n        \"repo_root\": discovered,\n        \"git_common_dir\": common,\n        \"git_dir\": git_dir,\n        \"head\": values[3].strip(),\n    }\n\n\ndef _commit_lane_snapshot(\n    repo_root: Path,\n    *,\n    message: str,\n    allow_empty: bool = False,\n    paths: Sequence[str] | None = None,\n    git: Callable[..., Any] | None = None,\n    git_output: Callable[..., str] | None = None,\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Commit lane output onto the current branch.\n\n    ``paths`` stages only the named repository-relative paths; ``None`` keeps\n    the historical stage-everything shape for the completed-lane persistence\n    path, whose scope verdict already ran.\n    \"\"\"\n    git_run = git if git is not None else _git\n    read_output = git_output if git_output is not None else _git_output\n    if paths is None:\n        staged = git_run(repo_root, \"add\", \"--all\", \"--\", \".\")\n    else:\n        staged = git_run(repo_root, \"add\", \"--\", *paths)\n    if staged.returncode != 0:\n        detail = str(\n            getattr(staged, \"stderr\", None) or getattr(staged, \"stdout\", None) or \"git add failed\"\n        ).strip()\n        raise TaskRunError(f\"git add failed: {detail}\")\n    commit_cmd = [\"commit\"]\n    if allow_empty:\n        commit_cmd.append(\"--allow-empty\")\n    commit_cmd.extend([\"--no-verify\", \"--message\", message])\n    committed = git_run(\n        repo_root,\n        \"-c\",\n        \"user.email=charness-task-run@localhost\",\n        \"-c\",\n        \"user.name=charness task run\",\n        *commit_cmd,\n    )\n    if committed.returncode != 0:\n        detail = str(\n            getattr(committed, \"stderr\", None)\n            or getattr(committed, \"stdout\", None)\n            or \"git commit failed\"\n        ).strip()\n        raise TaskRunError(f\"git commit failed: {detail}\")\n    sha = _head_sha_from_checkout(repo_root) if git is None else None\n    if not sha:\n        sha = read_output(repo_root, \"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\").strip()\n    return {\n        \"status\": \"committed\",\n        \"sha\": sha,\n        \"message\": message,\n        \"correctness_verified\": False,\n    }\n\n\ndef _commit_wip_candidate(repo_root: Path, paths: Sequence[str]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Checkpoint declared-scope lane output as an explicitly unverified WIP.\n\n    Only the named paths are staged, and the commit is never empty: a caller\n    with zero scoped changes must skip the commit instead of recording one\n    (#816). An empty stage fails the commit rather than recording nothing.\n    \"\"\"\n    return _commit_lane_snapshot(\n        repo_root,\n        message=WIP_CANDIDATE_COMMIT_MESSAGE,\n        paths=list(paths),\n    )\n\n\ndef _require_git_root(repo_root: Path) -> Path:\n    repo_root = repo_root.expanduser().resolve()\n    discovered = worktree_root_from_files(repo_root)\n    if discovered is None:\n        discovered = Path(_git_output(repo_root, \"rev-parse\", \"--show-toplevel\").strip()).resolve()\n    if discovered != repo_root:\n        raise TaskRunError(\n            f\"--repo-root must be the Git worktree root, not a subdirectory: {repo_root}\"\n        )\n    return repo_root\n\n\ndef _resolve_base_sha(repo_root: Path, base: str) -> str:\n    if not base.strip():\n        raise TaskRunError(\"--base is required and must resolve to a commit\")\n    result = _git(\n        repo_root,\n        \"rev-parse\",\n        \"--verify\",\n        \"--quiet\",\n        \"--end-of-options\",\n        f\"{base}^{{commit}}\",\n    )\n    sha = result.stdout.strip()\n    if result.returncode != 0 or not re.fullmatch(r\"[0-9a-fA-F]{40,64}\", sha):\n        raise TaskRunError(result.stderr.strip() or f\"ref is not resolvable: {base}\")\n    return sha\n\n\ndef _git_common_dir(repo_root: Path) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Writable Git administration root required for a linked worktree.\"\"\"\n    layout = layout_from_files(repo_root)\n    if layout is not None:\n        return layout.common_dir\n    value = _git_output(repo_root, \"rev-parse\", \"--git-common-dir\").strip()\n    common = Path(value)\n    if not common.is_absolute():\n        common = repo_root / common\n    resolved = common.resolve()\n    if not resolved.is_dir():\n        raise TaskRunError(f\"Git common directory is not a directory: {resolved}\")\n    return resolved\n\n\ndef _git_dir(repo_root: Path) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Return the checkout-specific Git administration directory.\"\"\"\n    layout = layout_from_files(repo_root)\n    if layout is not None:\n        return layout.git_dir\n    value = _git_output(repo_root, \"rev-parse\", \"--git-dir\").strip()\n    git_dir = Path(value)\n    if not git_dir.is_absolute():\n        git_dir = repo_root / git_dir\n    resolved = git_dir.resolve()\n    if not resolved.is_dir():\n        raise TaskRunError(f\"Git directory is not a directory: {resolved}\")\n    return resolved\n\n\ndef _validate_branch(_repo_root: Path, branch: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Named-branch spelling. Git's check-ref-format adds nothing the charset misses.\"\"\"\n    if (\n        not branch\n        or not _BRANCH_RE.fullmatch(branch)\n        or \"..\" in branch\n        or \"//\" in branch\n        or branch.endswith((\".\", \"/\", \".lock\"))\n        or \".lock/\" in branch\n    ):\n        raise TaskRunError(f\"--branch is not a valid named branch: {branch!r}\")\n    return branch\n\n\ndef _is_inside(path: Path, root: Path) -> bool:\n    try:\n        path.relative_to(root)\n    except ValueError:\n        return False\n    return True\n\n\ndef _validate_worktree_path(repo_root: Path, target_path: Path) -> Path:\n    target = target_path.expanduser().resolve()\n    if target == repo_root or _is_inside(target, repo_root):\n        raise TaskRunError(f\"worktree path must be outside the repository: {target}\")\n    if target.exists() or target.is_symlink():\n        raise TaskRunError(f\"worktree path already exists: {target}\")\n    return target\n\n\ndef _parse_nul_paths(output: str) -> list[str]:\n    return sorted({entry for entry in output.split(\"\\0\") if entry})\n\n\ndef _collect_populations(\n    repo_root: Path, *, checkout: CheckoutView | None = None\n) -> dict[str, list[str]]:\n    \"\"\"Worktree populations from the same porcelain-v2 snapshot as completion.\"\"\"\n    populations, _, _ = _collect_populations_with_metadata(repo_root, checkout=checkout)\n    return populations\n\n\ndef _collect_populations_with_metadata(\n    repo_root: Path, *, checkout: CheckoutView | None = None\n) -> tuple[dict[str, list[str]], str | None, str | None]:\n    \"\"\"Read one worktree moment: dirty populations plus the checked-out HEAD.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        moment = moment_from_status((checkout or GitCheckout(repo_root)).status(ignored=True))\n    except (GitStatusError, OSError) as exc:\n        raise TaskRunError(str(exc)) from exc\n    return moment.populations, moment.head_oid, moment.branch\n\n\ndef _snapshot_payload(snapshot: Mapping[str, Sequence[str]]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    return {key: {\"count\": len(paths), \"paths\": list(paths)} for key, paths in snapshot.items()}\n\n\ndef _population_delta(\n    before: Mapping[str, Sequence[str]],\n    after: Mapping[str, Sequence[str]],\n    *,\n    preflight: bool = False,\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    result: dict[str, Any] = {}\n    for population in (\"tracked\", \"untracked\", \"ignored\"):\n        before_set = set(before.get(population, ()))\n        after_set = set(after.get(population, ()))\n        added = sorted(after_set - before_set)\n        removed = sorted(before_set - after_set)\n        if preflight:\n            verdict = PASS if population == \"ignored\" or not after_set else FAIL\n            reason = (\n                \"ignored entries are reported as baseline residue\"\n                if population == \"ignored\"\n                else \"fresh worktrees must start without tracked or untracked changes\"\n            )\n        elif population == \"tracked\":\n            verdict, reason = PASS, \"tracked changes are the task candidate and remain inspectable\"\n        elif population == \"untracked\":\n            verdict = PASS\n            reason = (\n                \"new untracked files remain candidate changes; exact scope determines whether they are allowed\"\n                if added\n                else \"no new untracked files appeared during codex exec\"\n            )\n        else:\n            verdict = \"warn\" if added else PASS\n            reason = (\n                \"new ignored files appeared; inspect the generated-file causes\"\n                if added\n                else \"no new ignored files appeared during codex exec\"\n            )\n        result[population] = {\n            \"before_count\": len(before_set),\n            \"after_count\": len(after_set),\n            \"added\": added,\n            \"removed\": removed,\n            \"paths\": sorted(after_set),\n            \"verdict\": verdict,\n            \"reason\": reason,\n        }\n    return result\n\n\ndef _changed_paths(repo_root: Path, base_sha: str) -> list[str]:\n    return sorted(set(_diff_paths(repo_root, base_sha)) | set(_untracked_paths(repo_root)))\n\n\ndef _diff_paths(repo_root: Path, *revisions: str) -> list[str]:\n    return _parse_nul_paths(\n        _git_output(\n            repo_root,\n            \"diff\",\n            \"--no-renames\",\n            \"--name-only\",\n            \"-z\",\n            *revisions,\n            \"--\",\n        )\n    )\n\n\ndef _untracked_paths(repo_root: Path) -> list[str]:\n    return _parse_nul_paths(\n        _git_output(repo_root, \"ls-files\", \"--others\", \"--exclude-standard\", \"-z\", \"--\")\n    )\n\n\ndef _digest_frame(digest: Any, value: bytes) -> None:\n    digest.update(len(value).to_bytes(8, \"big\"))\n    digest.update(value)\n\n\ndef _candidate_content_digest(repo_root: Path, base_sha: str, changed_paths: Sequence[str]) -> str:\n    digest = hashlib.sha256()\n    _digest_frame(digest, b\"charness.task-run.candidate.v1\")\n    _digest_frame(digest, base_sha.encode(\"ascii\"))\n    for path in changed_paths:\n        _digest_frame(digest, os.fsencode(path))\n        candidate_path = repo_root / path\n        try:\n            metadata = candidate_path.lstat()\n        except FileNotFoundError:\n            _digest_frame(digest, b\"missing\")\n            continue\n        _digest_frame(digest, str(stat.S_IMODE(metadata.st_mode)).encode(\"ascii\"))\n        if stat.S_ISLNK(metadata.st_mode):\n            _digest_frame(digest, b\"symlink\")\n            _digest_frame(digest, os.fsencode(os.readlink(candidate_path)))\n        elif stat.S_ISREG(metadata.st_mode):\n            _digest_frame(digest, b\"file\")\n            _digest_frame(digest, candidate_path.read_bytes())\n        else:\n            _digest_frame(digest, b\"special\")\n            _digest_frame(digest, str(metadata.st_size).encode(\"ascii\"))\n    return digest.hexdigest()\n\n\ndef _is_ancestor(repo_root: Path, base_sha: str, head: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"True when `base_sha` is reachable from `head`, so HEAD can carry base-to-HEAD.\"\"\"\n    completed = run_process(\n        [\"git\", \"-C\", str(repo_root), \"merge-base\", \"--is-ancestor\", base_sha, head],\n        cwd=repo_root,\n        timeout_seconds=None,\n    )\n    return completed.returncode == 0\n\n\ndef _candidate_carrier(\n    repo_root: Path,\n    base_sha: str,\n    populations: Mapping[str, Sequence[str]] | None = None,\n    head: str | None = None,\n    branch: str | None = None,\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Describe which lane tree carries the complete validated candidate.\"\"\"\n    head = (\n        head\n        or _head_sha_from_checkout(repo_root)\n        or _git_output(repo_root, \"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\").strip()\n    )\n    # ANCESTRY, not inequality. `head != base_sha` answers \"did HEAD move\", which is a\n    # different question from \"does HEAD carry the base-to-worktree candidate\". A lane\n    # that amends its own base, or resets to an ancestor, leaves a clean tree at a\n    # SIBLING commit -- and the inequality test called that `commit-only` with\n    # `head_is_complete: true`, which invites the parent to cherry-pick a commit that\n    # replays against the wrong parent instead of carrying the validated candidate.\n    # Equality already establishes ancestry.  Most worktree-only task runs use\n    # ``base=HEAD`` and used to pay for a merge-base subprocess before asking\n    # Git for the same tracked diff twice below.\n    base_is_ancestor = head == base_sha or _is_ancestor(repo_root, base_sha, head)\n    has_commit = head != base_sha and base_is_ancestor\n    committed_paths = _diff_paths(repo_root, base_sha, head) if has_commit else []\n    # Porcelain status is the one coherent snapshot of the current worktree\n    # population.  Its tracked and untracked paths are exactly the dirty\n    # populations needed below; asking Git separately for `diff HEAD` and\n    # `ls-files --others` only re-reads that same boundary.  Keep the\n    # base-relative diff below because status cannot answer whether a path was\n    # restored to the selected base after a lane commit.\n    current_populations = populations or _collect_populations(repo_root)\n    untracked_paths = list(current_populations[\"untracked\"])\n    working_tree_paths = sorted(set(current_populations[\"tracked\"]) | set(untracked_paths))\n    if head == base_sha:\n        # The two tracked views are identical when HEAD is the selected base;\n        # status is the complete candidate view, so no separate diff or\n        # untracked listing is necessary.\n        changed_paths = working_tree_paths\n        dirty_paths = list(working_tree_paths)\n    else:\n        dirty_paths = working_tree_paths\n        if has_commit and not dirty_paths:\n            # A clean descendant HEAD is exactly the committed candidate already\n            # read above. Re-running the same base diff cannot add information.\n            changed_paths = list(committed_paths)\n        else:\n            changed_paths = sorted(set(_diff_paths(repo_root, base_sha)) | set(untracked_paths))\n    if not has_commit:\n        carrier_kind = \"worktree-only\"\n    elif dirty_paths:\n        carrier_kind = \"commit-plus-dirty\"\n    else:\n        carrier_kind = \"commit-only\"\n    return {\n        \"changed_paths\": changed_paths,\n        \"carrier_kind\": carrier_kind,\n        \"committed_paths\": committed_paths,\n        \"dirty_paths\": dirty_paths,\n        \"head_sha\": head if has_commit else None,\n        # Published even when it is True, because its FALSE case is otherwise\n        # invisible: a lane that amended its base has a clean tree at a sibling\n        # commit, and without this the receipt reads exactly like a lane that never\n        # committed at all. A parent that sees `observed_head` differ from the base\n        # while this is False knows a commit exists and does not carry the candidate.\n        \"base_is_ancestor_of_head\": base_is_ancestor,\n        \"observed_head_sha\": head,\n        \"observed_branch\": branch,\n        \"head_is_complete\": has_commit and not dirty_paths,\n        \"content_digest\": _candidate_content_digest(repo_root, base_sha, changed_paths),\n    }\n\n\ndef _checkout_own_dir(create_payload: dict[str, Any]) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Validate the checkout-specific Git dir carried by worktree creation.\"\"\"\n    carrier = create_payload.get(\"_checkout\")\n    if not isinstance(carrier, dict):\n        raise TaskRunError(\"worktree create payload is missing checkout metadata\")\n    raw = carrier.get(\"own_dir\")\n    if not isinstance(raw, str) or not raw or raw != raw.strip():\n        raise TaskRunError(\"worktree create payload has malformed checkout own_dir\")\n    candidate = Path(raw)\n    if not candidate.is_absolute():\n        raise TaskRunError(\"worktree create payload checkout own_dir must be absolute\")\n    try:\n        resolved = candidate.resolve()\n    except (OSError, RuntimeError, ValueError) as exc:\n        raise TaskRunError(\"worktree create payload has unusable checkout own_dir\") from exc\n    if not resolved.is_dir():\n        raise TaskRunError(\n            f\"worktree create payload checkout own_dir is not an existing directory: {resolved}\"\n        )\n    return resolved\n",

    "size_bytes": 20275,

  {
    "carrier_path": "charness-artifacts/critique/workers/v893-release-critique-a/semantic-input/0046-content.bin",
    "carrier_sha256": "5ff6311b8b47b0e2745ff78f205ab819f84c1d52a9c5af1055042bdd932cea3d",
    "content_sha256": "5ff6311b8b47b0e2745ff78f205ab819f84c1d52a9c5af1055042bdd932cea3d",
    "disposition": "present",
    "path": "scripts/task_run/task_run_lane_runner.py",
    "prompt_content": "\"\"\"Lane runner command construction and receipt recording.\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any, Sequence\n\n\ndef _load_repo_runtime_bootstrap():\n    pathlib, sys = __import__(\"pathlib\"), __import__(\"sys\")\n    marker = (\"scripts\", \"adapter_lib.py\")\n    parents = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parents\n    root = next((p for p in parents if p.joinpath(*marker).is_file()), None)\n    if root is not None and str(root) not in sys.path:\n        sys.path.insert(0, str(root))\n\n\n_load_repo_runtime_bootstrap()\n\nfrom scripts.task_run import task_run_support as _support  # noqa: E402\n\nbuild_codex_command = _support.build_codex_command\nbuild_muse_command = _support.build_muse_command\n\nLANE_PHASES = (\"CONTRACT-READ\", \"EDITING\", \"TESTING\")\n\n\ndef build_lane_prompt(\n    prompt: str, *, require_change: bool, scopes: Sequence[str]\n) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Shape the lane prompt; implementation lanes get carrier directives.\n\n    A require-change lane once spent a full model run in analysis without a\n    scoped edit (#815): the user prompt used to travel verbatim, so nothing\n    told the executor it was in an implementation lane. Require-change lanes\n    now name the scope, demand prompt entry into the edit loop, and define a\n    typed early blocker; all other lanes pass through untouched.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not require_change:\n        return prompt\n    scope_list = \", \".join(scopes)\n    return (\n        \"[charness task-run: implementation lane]\\n\"\n        f\"Scope: {scope_list}. This is an implementation lane, not a critique lane.\\n\"\n        \"After the minimum contract reads, enter the scoped edit/test loop \"\n        \"promptly and prioritize producing the first scoped diff.\\n\"\n        \"Emit progress lines as you go, one per line: CONTRACT-READ when \"\n        \"contract reads are done, EDITING when the first scoped edit lands, \"\n        \"TESTING when verification runs.\\n\"\n        \"If you cannot make a scoped change, stop promptly and reply with a \"\n        \"typed blocker on its own line: BLOCKED: <concrete reason>. Do not \"\n        \"consume the run in further analysis once blocked.\\n\"\n        \"---\\n\"\n        f\"{prompt}\"\n    )\n\n\ndef lane_progress(stdout_text: str) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Parse phase markers and the typed blocker from lane stdout (#815).\"\"\"\n    phases: list[str] = []\n    blocker: str | None = None\n    for line in stdout_text.splitlines():\n        stripped = line.strip()\n        if stripped in LANE_PHASES and stripped not in phases:\n            phases.append(stripped)\n        elif blocker is None and stripped.startswith(\"BLOCKED:\"):\n            blocker = stripped[len(\"BLOCKED:\"):].strip() or None\n    return {\"phases\": phases, \"blocker\": blocker}\n\n\ndef lane_receipt_blockers(\n    *,\n    progress: dict[str, Any],\n    require_change: bool,\n    scope: Any,\n) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Blocker lines naming how a finished lane stalled, if it did (#815).\n\n    A typed `BLOCKED:` line becomes a `lane reported blocker` entry so the\n    receipt and next step carry the lane's own reason. A changeless\n    require-change lane that never emitted `EDITING` names that stall\n    instead; lanes that edited or changed scope need no lane entry.\n    \"\"\"\n    if progress[\"blocker\"] is not None:\n        return [f\"lane reported blocker: {progress['blocker']}\"]\n    if (\n        require_change\n        and not scope.get(\"changed_paths\")\n        and not scope.get(\"disallowed_paths\")\n        and \"EDITING\" not in progress[\"phases\"]\n    ):\n        return [\n            \"lane stalled: require-change lane ended without EDITING and no scoped change\"\n        ]\n    return []\n\n\ndef apply_lane_receipt(\n    payload: dict[str, Any],\n    blockers: list[str],\n    *,\n    delivery: Any,\n    require_change: bool,\n    scope: Any,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Record `lane_progress` on the receipt and append lane stall blockers.\"\"\"\n    progress = lane_progress(delivery.get(\"text\") or \"\")\n    payload[\"lane_progress\"] = progress\n    blockers.extend(\n        lane_receipt_blockers(progress=progress, require_change=require_change, scope=scope)\n    )\n\n\ndef prepare_lane_execution(\n    payload: dict[str, Any],\n    resolved: dict[str, Any],\n    git_worktree_dir: Path,\n    execution_runtime_path: Path,\n    *,\n    prompt: str,\n    require_change: bool,\n    scopes: Sequence[str],\n    executor: str,\n    executable: str,\n    effort: str,\n    worktree: Path,\n) -> tuple[list[Path], str, list[str]]:\n    \"\"\"Resolve sandbox grants, shape the lane prompt, and build its command.\n\n    One call prepares everything the lane runner needs to execute: writable\n    grants (recorded on the receipt), the shaped prompt (implementation\n    directives for require-change lanes, verbatim otherwise), and the\n    executor command carrying that prompt.\n    \"\"\"\n    writable_dirs = lane_writable_dirs(\n        payload,\n        resolved,\n        git_worktree_dir,\n        execution_runtime_path,\n        executor=executor,\n        worktree=worktree,\n    )\n    lane_prompt = build_lane_prompt(\n        prompt, require_change=require_change, scopes=scopes\n    )\n    command = lane_command(\n        executor=executor,\n        executable=executable,\n        effort=effort,\n        prompt=lane_prompt,\n        execution_runtime_path=execution_runtime_path,\n        writable_dirs=writable_dirs,\n        worktree=worktree,\n    )\n    return writable_dirs, lane_prompt, command\n\n\ndef _checkpoint_interrupted_lane(\n    resolved_target: Path, base_sha: str, scope_specs: list[dict[str, Any]]\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Commit declared-scope changes as the WIP candidate, or record the skip.\n\n    Stale harness residue or an empty lane must not become a candidate commit\n    (#816). The scope verdict in completion classifies the same population, so\n    this reuses its refreshed specs rather than redefining them.\n    \"\"\"\n    refreshed_specs = _support._refresh_scope_specs(resolved_target, scope_specs)\n    changed = _support._candidate_carrier(resolved_target, base_sha)[\"changed_paths\"]\n    scoped = _support._paths_in_scopes(changed, refreshed_specs)\n    if not scoped:\n        return {\n            \"status\": \"skipped\",\n            \"reason\": \"no scoped changes: no WIP candidate commit created\",\n            \"changed_paths\": [],\n            \"correctness_verified\": False,\n        }\n    return _support._commit_wip_candidate(resolved_target, scoped)\n\n\ndef lane_writable_dirs(\n    payload: dict[str, Any],\n    resolved: dict[str, Any],\n    git_worktree_dir: Path,\n    execution_runtime_path: Path,\n    *,\n    executor: str,\n    worktree: Path,\n) -> list[Path]:\n    \"\"\"Sandbox grants the lane runner honors, recorded on the receipt.\n\n    Codex lanes get workspace-write plus `--add-dir` grants beyond the\n    worktree itself: its sandbox holds a workdir's `.agents/` read-only\n    (measured 2026-09-02), so the worktree's `.agents/` is granted when\n    present. Muse lanes root their single `--workspace` at the lane\n    worktree itself and take no `--add-dir` grants (#814); the receipt\n    records that root as `workspace` instead of `writable_dirs`.\n    \"\"\"\n    if executor != \"codex\":\n        payload[\"workspace\"] = str(worktree)\n        return []\n    writable_dirs = [resolved[\"git_common_dir\"], git_worktree_dir, execution_runtime_path]\n    worktree_agents_dir = Path(payload[\"worktree_path\"]) / \".agents\"\n    if worktree_agents_dir.is_dir():\n        writable_dirs.append(worktree_agents_dir)\n    payload[\"writable_dirs\"] = [str(path) for path in writable_dirs]\n    return writable_dirs\n\n\ndef lane_command(\n    *,\n    executor: str,\n    executable: str,\n    effort: str,\n    prompt: str,\n    execution_runtime_path: Path,\n    writable_dirs: Sequence[Path],\n    worktree: Path,\n) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Build the lane runner command for the selected executor.\n\n    Codex lanes read the prompt from stdin (`-`); `muse exec` takes no stdin\n    prompt, so muse lanes carry it via a prompt file in the lane-private\n    execution root. Muse lanes root their single `--workspace` at the lane\n    worktree itself (#814); the Codex `--add-dir` grants in `writable_dirs`\n    do not apply to them.\n    \"\"\"\n    if executor == \"muse\":\n        prompt_file = execution_runtime_path / \"prompt.md\"\n        prompt_file.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        prompt_file.write_text(prompt, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        return build_muse_command(\n            executable,\n            effort=effort,\n            prompt_file=prompt_file,\n            worktree=worktree,\n        )\n    return build_codex_command(\n        executable,\n        effort=effort,\n        writable_dirs=writable_dirs,\n    )\n\n\ndef record_lane_runner(\n    payload: dict[str, Any],\n    *,\n    executor: str,\n    executable: str,\n    effort: str,\n    timeout_seconds: int,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Record the lane runner block and its legacy `codex` alias.\"\"\"\n    block = {\n        \"kind\": executor,\n        \"executable\": executable,\n        \"model\": _support.TASK_MODEL if executor == \"codex\" else _support.TASK_MUSE_MODEL,\n        \"effort\": effort,\n        \"timeout_seconds\": timeout_seconds,\n        \"timeout_scope\": f\"{executor}-exec\",\n    }\n    payload[\"executor\"] = block\n    if executor == \"codex\":\n        # Legacy alias: result.json readers address the runner as \"codex\".\n        payload[\"codex\"] = block\n",
    "prompt_encoding": "utf-8",

    "path": "skills/public/quality/references/attention-state-visibility.json",
    "prompt_content": "{\n  \"_purpose\": \"Declare every repo-owned Python command/helper surface that contains exit-zero attention-state terms. The gate prevents new no_adapter/disabled/not_configured/skipped/prose-review-required states from silently reading as clean pass states.\",\n  \"_contract\": \"Every detected file must declare its exact states, at least one visibility class, evidence_terms that appear in the source, and a rationale. Use local_noop_not_closeout only for runtime/internal no-op payloads that are not standing quality or release closeout proof.\",\n  \"_visibility_values\": [\n    \"artifact_visible\",\n    \"explicit_skip_summary\",\n    \"hard_failure_or_blocker\",\n    \"local_noop_not_closeout\",\n    \"metric_or_trace\",\n    \"operator_summary\",\n    \"stdout_attention\",\n    \"structured_warning\",\n    \"support_trace\",\n    \"terminal_payload\"\n  ],\n  \"files\": {\n    \"scripts/gates_support/announcement_verification_lib.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"artifact_visible\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"skipped\",\n        \"reason\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"resolve_manual_disposition returns a TYPED, RECORDED `skipped` disposition -- the fallback path when no adapter `post_delivery_readback_probe` is declared for `human-backend` announcement delivery. record_announcement.py writes it into the delivery record's `verification` field in `.charness/announcement/announcements.jsonl`. This satisfies the rung-1 presence floor (a confirmation or a typed non-`verified` disposition pass equally, mirroring the release skill's WS-1 rung-1/rung-2 split) but is never a silent clean pass -- the disposition and its `reason` are legible in the persisted ledger record that announcement closeout renders from.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/evidence/boundary_probe_lib.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"not_configured\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"structured_warning\",\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"PROBE_NOT_CONFIGURED\",\n        \"not-configured\",\n        \"state\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"`not_configured` is one arm of the three-state probe vocabulary `evaluated | not-configured | not-established` that replaced a bare `triggered: false`. It is a DETERMINED verdict, not a silent pass: the repo deliberately declared no cross-surface probe, so the objective override is off by design while the always-brief and closeout presence floors still apply. It is distinguishable from `not-established` (configured but not evaluable) on two channels at once — the state word and the exit byte, since undetermined exits 3 and this exits 0. The state leads the payload and `triggered` is emitted ONLY when the probe actually ran, so a caller polling one key cannot read 'could not tell' as 'no'.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/retro_debug/build_debug_seam_risk_index.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"skipped_artifacts\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"Skipped debug artifacts are listed in the index payload with reasons and are not treated as clean seam-risk proof.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/lessons/build_retro_lesson_selection_index.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"not_configured\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"summary_projection\",\n        \"not_configured\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"`summary_path: null` is the retro adapter's declared opt-out of the Markdown digest. The command still writes or validates the source-linked selection index and validates the ledger; its payload names the digest comparison as `summary_projection: not_configured` so a reader can tell a declared absence from a comparison that ran.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/gates/check_cli_skill_surface.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"status\",\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"The release/quality CLI surface check returns an explicit skipped status and reason when no relevant surface changed.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/gates/check_command_docs.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"missing-contract\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"Repos without command-doc contracts get an explicit skipped status and reason.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/gates/check_consumer_validator_catalog.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"not_configured\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"structured_warning\",\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"not_configured\",\n        \"consumer-validator adoption declaration is missing\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"An inventory target without the consumer-validator adoption declaration is explicit `not_configured` state, not a clean catalog pass; the CLI payload and nonzero result keep the missing adoption contract visible.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/gates/check_coverage_lib.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"not_configured\",\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"not-configured\",\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"Coverage scenarios preserve the explicit absent-healthcheck state and skipped marker in the lifecycle payload.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/gates/check_documented_command_flags.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"operator_summary\",\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"A documented invocation this gate cannot resolve to a runnable script -- a <placeholder> path for a consuming repo, or a path check_doc_links.py owns -- is counted by reason instead of dropped. render_report delegates to gate_report_emit.render_findings_with_skipped, which appends the \\\"Not proven\\\" count and every reason to the PASS output as well as the fail output; the same map rides in the YAML payload as skipped, so a green run states how much of the documented command surface it did not prove rather than reading as full coverage.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/gates/check_documented_subcommands.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"operator_summary\",\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"A documented `charness` invocation whose subcommand token is not a single subcommand name -- `charness tool install/update/doctor` names three of them in one prose span -- is counted by reason instead of guessed at. render_report delegates to gate_report_emit.render_findings_with_skipped, which appends the \\\"Not proven\\\" count and every reason to the PASS output as well as the fail output; the same map rides in the YAML payload as skipped, so a green run states how much of the documented subcommand surface it did not prove rather than reading as full coverage.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/mutation/check_mutation_score.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"operator_summary\",\n        \"metric_or_trace\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"Mutation skipped workers remain counted in the score metrics and are rendered by the summary helper.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/mutation/check_mutation_score_summary_lib.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"operator_summary\",\n        \"metric_or_trace\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"Skipped:\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"Mutation skipped workers are rendered in the printed score summary.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/gates/check_prescribed_skill_executed_lib.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\",\n        \"hard_failure_or_blocker\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"skipped\",\n        \"host-blocked-subagent\",\n        \"host-log-not-exposed\",\n        \"evaluator-unavailable\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"Shared closeout helper library carries the same enum-bounded skip semantics into structured ok/blockers payloads consumed by achieve/issue/release closeout gates.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/gates/check_seed_fixture_budget.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"advisory-only\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"stdout_attention\",\n        \"structured_warning\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"advisory_only_no_pytest_temp_yet\",\n        \"advisory_only_du_unavailable\",\n        \"advisory_only_scan_failure_waived\",\n        \"blocking_pytest_temp_scan_failed\",\n        \"scope_classification\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"Seed fixture budget gate reports advisory-only for the three states where no measurement is possible or the block was explicitly waived (no pytest tmp directory yet, a `du` this box cannot run, or --advisory-on-scan-failure). A scan that failed for any other reason is BLOCKING, not advisory: a run that measured nothing must not read as a pass. The payload exposes the classification, the scan reason, and the attempt count.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/gates/check_test_production_ratio.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"structured_warning\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"skipped_paths\",\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"The ratio gate reports undecodable Python sources (the committed malformed repograph parser fixtures) as TYPED skipped paths with counts in its output instead of crashing; the paths are listed so the exclusion is visible, and the gate's pass/fail verdict is computed over the decodable corpus only.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/gates/check_upstream_support_drift.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"skipped (\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"Skipped upstream checks are surfaced as per-record errors in the drift report.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/gates_support/classify_t_signal.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"skipped_reason\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"T-signal classifier exposes skipped reasons (no_parent, shallow_clone, diff_unavailable) in its structured result so callers can attach classification_skipped to the durable episode record instead of silently emitting t_status none.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/adapters/control_plane_lifecycle_lib.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"not_configured\",\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"not-configured\",\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"Tool lifecycle payloads distinguish an absent optional healthcheck from a successful healthcheck with explicit status and skipped fields.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/review/critique_enforcement_scope.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"disabled\",\n        \"not_configured\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"stdout_attention\",\n        \"operator_summary\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"binding-currency-check=\",\n        \"disabled (a full sweep\",\n        \"not-resolved\",\n        \"not-established\",\n        \"not-configured\",\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"This module exists to stop exactly this failure on the critique evidence surface: a conditional floor that is off emits nothing by construction, so `Validated N critique artifact(s).` read as coverage it never claimed. Every state it names is printed on stdout in the enforcement-scope record on BOTH passing and failing runs — `binding-currency-check=disabled` when a full sweep deliberately skips re-reading historical bindings, and the typed cross-surface states `not-configured` / `not-established` / `not-resolved`, which the bare boolean previously collapsed into an indistinguishable `False`. None of them is a pass verdict; each names a scope the run did not establish.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/setup/doctor.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"disabled\",\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"install_method\",\n        \"disabled\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"The setup package's consumer doctor reports disabled tool installs and skipped release probes as structured JSON. The literal lives here rather than in each gate, which is why the callers declare the `skipped` state and this file declares the term that renders it.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/gates_support/github_actions_lib.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"metric_or_trace\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"Skipped workflow steps are parsed as source facts for downstream gates, not closeout proof.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/hooks/host_hook_install_lib.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"disabled\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"intent\",\n        \"disabled\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"Host-hook install/uninstall library exposes per-host intent (enabled/disabled) in reconcile and status payloads so adapter-driven no-op runs cannot read as silent install.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/gates/measure_inventory_marker_rule.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"metric_or_trace\",\n        \"explicit_skip_summary\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"prose_review_status\",\n        \"pre_contract_citations_skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"The measurement reports every pre-contract citation it skipped as a named list rather than dropping it silently, and states in its non-claims that the gate's prose_review_status arm is not modelled.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/mutation/mutation_test_reporters.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"skipped\",\n        \"evidence\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"`skipped` is a verdict-critical count read from one structurally complete Node TAP summary, not an internal clean-pass branch. Uncomparable paths are counted per path with the reason, printed on the success line, and carried under `uncomparable` in the YAML payload, so a consumer can distinguish an empty result from an unexamined one. (2) `library_unavailable`: when `scripts/evidence/probe_record_lib.py` cannot be resolved from this tree the floor returns `ok: false` with the reason text -- it REFUSES rather than skipping, because a check that could not run has not run, and reporting it as satisfied is the class of silence this floor exists to close. Both are `scope_classification` values on the returned payload, and each ships a populated `reason` naming what was not scanned and how to configure it, so the state travels in the inventory payload rather than as a silent clean verdict.\"\n    },\n    \"skills/public/quality/scripts/inventory_cli_ergonomics.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"advisory-only\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"stdout_attention\",\n        \"structured_warning\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"advisory-only\",\n        \"scope_classification\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"CLI ergonomics inventory prints an advisory-only scope_classification (carried from cli_ergonomics_lib) so green output cannot be read as enforcement.\"\n    },\n    \"skills/public/quality/scripts/inventory_skill_ergonomics.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"prose_review_status\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"structured_warning\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"prose_review_status\",\n        \"advisories\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"The compact skill ergonomics summary carries prose-review-required state and the advisory list so reviewers do not need the full per-skill payload to see the non-blocking attention state.\"\n    },\n    \"skills/public/quality/scripts/quality_declaration_lifecycle.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"not_configured\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"operator_summary\",\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"not-configured\",\n        \"gaps\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"The declaration lifecycle is embedded in the planner payload, and the prescribed default human rendering prints its status, every gap, every routed command, and each not-run execution state. The post-publish verification renderers (and the `skipped` state they must render honestly) moved to publish_release_verification_sections.py.\"\n    },\n    \"skills/public/release/scripts/publish_release_post_create.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"not_configured\",\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\",\n        \"artifact_visible\",\n        \"local_noop_not_closeout\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"refreshed\",\n        \"failed\",\n        \"skipped\",\n        \"reason\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"run_post_publish_install_refresh returns not_configured (surfaced under install_refresh). confirm_release_via_distinct_channel (WS-1 rung-2) returns status skipped only when there is no published release URL and no adapter post_publish_distinct_channel_probe; that skipped is a TYPED, RECORDED disposition written to payload.distinct_channel_verification and rendered in the release artifact's `## Distinct-Channel Verification` section, which the human rung-2 disposition review audits. Not a runtime status of this script -- a status it refuses to paper over.\"\n    },\n    \"skills/public/release/scripts/resolve_adapter.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"advisory-only\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"requested_review_policy\",\n        \"review skipped because\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"Release adapter parsing preserves requested-review advisory-only policy and skipped-review patterns for requested-review gate decisions.\"\n    },\n    \"skills/public/retro/scripts/check_auto_trigger.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"not_configured\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"structured_warning\",\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"configuration_status\",\n        \"not-configured\",\n        \"state\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"Same three-state probe vocabulary as `boundary_probe_lib`: `not_configured` means this repo declared no auto-retro trigger, which is a determined opt-out and exits 0, while an adapter that is absent, misspelled, or unparseable is `not-established` and exits 3. Before that split a caller polling `triggered` read every one of those worlds as a judged 'no'.\"\n    },\n    \"skills/public/setup/scripts/seed_dependencies.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"status\",\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"Dependency seeding reports skipped dependency setup as structured payload status.\"\n    },\n    \"skills/public/shared/scripts/reviewer_result.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"skipped_oversized_records\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"The transcript reader caps per-record size so a pathological reviewer message cannot overflow the caller's context, and counts the records it skipped for that reason. This is a diagnostic helper's read budget, not a quality or release closeout signal: the helper is explicitly the diagnostic floor for a stranded reviewer result, and every payload carries diagnostic_only=true plus the contract sentence saying so.\"\n    },\n    \"skills/support/markdown-preview/scripts/render_markdown_preview.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"disabled\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"structured_warning\",\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"Markdown preview is disabled by config.\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"Markdown preview disabled state includes a warning in the render payload.\"\n    },\n    \"skills/support/web-fetch/scripts/acquisition_trace_lib.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"support_trace\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"status=\\\"skipped\\\"\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"Web-fetch skipped acquisition attempts are explicit trace entries.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/task_run/task_run_lane_runner.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"skipped\",\n        \"reason\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"An interrupted lane with zero declared-scope changes records candidate commit `status: skipped` with a `reason` instead of an empty WIP commit. It is a typed, persisted field in the lane's terminal `result.json`, and the timed-out next-step names the missing commit, so a skipped checkpoint cannot read as a recorded candidate.\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/task_run/task_run_completion.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"terminal_payload\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"skipped\",\n        \"reason\",\n        \"changed_line_gate\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"_changed_line_verdict records `changed_line_gate.status: skipped` with a `reason` when a lane ended without a validated candidate, so there is nothing for the changed-line gate to judge. It is a typed, persisted field in the lane's terminal `result.json` beside `not-run` (no gate supplied) and `not-applicable` (a tree without the gate script); none of the three is `clean`, `blocking` is false only because no candidate exists, and the parent reads the status at the receipt (docs/agent-task-runs.md).\"\n    },\n    \"scripts/gates_support/runtime_root_retention.py\": {\n      \"states\": [\n        \"skipped\"\n      ],\n      \"visibility\": [\n        \"artifact_visible\",\n        \"stdout_attention\"\n      ],\n      \"evidence_terms\": [\n        \"skipped\",\n        \"reason\",\n        \"retention\"\n      ],\n      \"rationale\": \"The runtime-root sweep records `skipped` for every entry it deliberately left alone (a running lane, a key or subtree touched within the active window, a live pytest run lock, an unverifiable salvage), each with its reason, in the JSON log under `<key>/retention/` and, for the by-hand CLI, on stderr; the standing-runner hook prints the counts on one summary line. A skip is a retention decision the operator can read, never a claim that anything was cleaned.\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n",

    "carrier_path": "charness-artifacts/critique/workers/v893-release-critique-a/semantic-input/0048-content.bin",

    "disposition": "present",
    "path": "skills/public/release/references/adapter-contract.md",
    "prompt_content": "# Release Adapter Contract\n\n`release` stays portable by loading repo-specific version and install-surface\nseams from a repo adapter.\n\n## Canonical Path\n\nUse `<repo-root>/.agents/release-adapter.yaml`.\n\n## Shared Core\n\n- `version`\n- `repo`\n- `language`\n- `output_dir`\n- `preset_id`\n- `preset_version`\n- `customized_from`\n\n## Release Fields\n\n- `package_id`\n- `packaging_manifest_path`\n- `materialized_plugin_root`\n- `sync_command`\n- `quality_command`\n- `post_publish_install_refresh`\n- `post_publish_version_readback`\n- `post_publish_doctor_readback`\n- `post_publish_distinct_channel_probe`\n- `update_instructions`\n- `requested_review_commands`\n- `requested_review_policy`\n- `review_unavailable_patterns`\n- `review_waiver_phrases`\n- `product_surfaces`\n- `cli_skill_surface_probe_commands`\n- `cli_skill_surface_command_docs`\n- `cli_skill_surface_skill_paths`\n- `cli_skill_surface_change_globs`\n- `fresh_checkout_probes`\n- `required_release_surfaces`\n- `unpublished_release_surfaces`\n- `require_derived_release_claims`\n- `release_backend`\n- `specialized_release_lanes`\n\n## Executed fields\n\n`sync_command` and `quality_command` are RUN by a subprocess; every other field in\nthis contract is READ. That split is why their defaults below are the one place a\nplaceholder would be wrong: a documentation placeholder in a read field is correct\nand in an executed field is a broken command.\n\nBoth defaults name the AUTHORING repo's own tooling, so a consuming repo that never\nwrote a release adapter inherits commands that cannot exist in its tree. Adapter\nresolution warns when it can read the script path out of either value and that path\nis missing, naming the field and whether the value was inferred or set. `bump_version`\ngoes further and REFUSES on a missing `sync_command` target before it writes the\nversion, because that command runs after the manifest is written and a failure there\nwould leave a bumped manifest with an unsynced plugin mirror.\n\nThe reader is narrow on purpose: it resolves only `python3 <relative-path>` and\n`./<relative-path>` where the path uses `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]` and nothing else. Every other\nshape is silent — a pipeline or chain in any spelling, a redirect, an env prefix, a\ndifferent interpreter, an interpreter option, a quoted, tilde, globbed or\nbrace-expanded path. Silence means it did not judge, not that the command is fine. Set\nboth fields to commands YOUR repo can run.\n\n## Quality scheduling\n\nExecute appends `--release-prepare` only to a simple native\n`./scripts/run-quality.sh --release` invocation (optionally `--full`, `--read-only`,\nor `--review`). Prepare runs all selected gates except `pytest-release`, replacing\nthe last-release receipt with an **unestablished** result; storage failure fails\npreparation. Resume runs the ordinary full release lane before publication.\nPreparation cannot seal a final push receipt or satisfy the local commit floor.\nCustom commands, pipelines and chains retain full execution at both stages.\nDo not put `--release-prepare` in the adapter: execute owns that choice.\n\n## Defaults\n\n- `language`: `en`\n- `output_dir`: `<repo-root>/charness-artifacts/release`\n- `package_id`: repo directory name\n- `packaging_manifest_path`: `<repo-root>/packaging/<repo>.json`\n- `materialized_plugin_root`: `<repo-root>/plugins/<package_id>`\n- `sync_command`: `python3 scripts/plugin_export/sync_root_plugin_manifests.py --repo-root .`\n- `quality_command`: `<repo-root>/scripts/run-quality.sh`. Repos that gate\n  release-time regression tests behind a pytest marker (e.g., `release_only`)\n  should override this to include the marker — e.g.,\n  `./scripts/run-quality.sh --release` — so publish covers update/install\n  flow checks that standing pre-push intentionally skips.\n  A repo may make one release's changed-line evidence an explicit non-claim with\n  `./scripts/run-quality.sh --release --non-claim=release-changed-line-coverage`.\n  This exact release-only label omits that lane rather than converting a failed\n  verdict into success; all other release gates still run. Preserve the command\n  in the tagged adapter and state the missing verdict in the release notes.\n  When this repo-owned release command produces an established semantic receipt,\n  the resume helper seals it to the exact clean HEAD/tree and materialized export.\n  The child push may reuse that one-shot receipt for the pre-push broad lane; the\n  close-keyword guard always runs, and any state drift falls back to the normal\n  pre-push gate.\n- `update_instructions`: empty list\n- `requested_review_commands`: empty list\n- `requested_review_policy`: `warn-if-unconfigured`\n- `review_unavailable_patterns`: common release-record phrases such as\n  `review unavailable`, `review gate unavailable`, and `executor_variants`\n- `review_waiver_phrases`: `review waiver:`, `explicit review waiver:`, and\n  `requested review waiver:`\n- `specialized_release_lanes`: empty list. An optional list of mappings with\n  `id`, `workflow`, `tag_pattern`, and `command` strings. A non-empty list is an\n  explicit declaration that this repository's generic release route does not\n  apply; the release planner reports the lane (or asks the operator to choose\n  when several are declared) and does not execute its command. This is local\n  declaration evidence only: hosted workflow state, tag triggers, and release\n  approval remain outside this planner's claim. Report the declared `command`\n  verbatim and never recompose its argument forwarding: the separator shape\n  belongs to the target repo's declared script, not to the planner. Rewriting\n  `pnpm run release:candidate:dispatch --source-sha <sha>` with an assumed\n  ` -- ` separator hands the script a literal leading `--` it rejects.\n- `product_surfaces`: empty list\n- `cli_skill_surface_*`: empty lists\n- `fresh_checkout_probes`: empty list\n- `required_release_surfaces`: empty list. Names the generated release surfaces the\n  repo asserts it publishes, so an ABSENT one becomes drift instead of reading like a\n  matching one. Known names: `claude_plugin`, `codex_plugin`,\n  `claude_marketplace_version`, `codex_marketplace_source_path`. The packaging\n  manifest is NOT declarable — its absence is drift whether or not anyone declares it.\n- `unpublished_release_surfaces`: empty list. The OPT-OUT channel, and deliberately not\n  an overload of the field above: because `required_release_surfaces` means \"these must\n  exist\", naming a surface you do not publish there makes it drift, so it cannot be the\n  remedy for not publishing it. Name here the generated surfaces this repo does not\n  ship. Same known names.\n- `require_derived_release_claims`: `true`. Notes handed to publish via\n  `--notes-file` must carry a generated derived-claim block that agrees with the tree\n  being shipped, and their authored prose must not carry an ungrounded quantity.\n  Generate and check with\n  `python3 \"$SKILL_DIR/scripts/generate_release_notes.py\" --repo-root . --notes-file <notes.md> --sync|--check`.\n  **The default is `true` on purpose, and the direction is the point.** A gate armed by\n  an opt-IN line is disarmed by deleting that line with nothing red; defaulting to true\n  inverts it, so deleting the key — or the whole adapter — RE-ARMS the gate and the only\n  way to publish unguarded notes is to write the opt-out down where a reviewer sees it.\n  Setting it `false` disarms BOTH arms: the derived-block requirement and prose\n  containment. Two things it does not do: it never runs on the resume lane (the window\n  between the prepared stop and the resume is closed to worktree changes, so a refusal\n  there would have no legal remedy), and it is never reached by a `--generate-notes`\n  publish, which supplies no notes file for it to read.\n\n- **Absence is still never drift on its own.** A read-only `current_release` run never\n  reddens a lane a consumer chose not to publish. What changed (D48) is that an absent\n  surface named by NEITHER field makes `absence_corroboration` read `uncorroborated`,\n  and `publish_release_preflight.release_surface_blocker` refuses the PUBLISH — the\n  irreversible boundary — rather than letting a deleted declaration buy a silent green.\n- A surface that is present but `unreadable` or missing its version IS drift without\n  `required_release_surfaces`, because that is the state a failed sync leaves and a\n  deleted declaration must not disarm it. It is still exemptable via\n  `unpublished_release_surfaces`, and that matters more than it looks: the two\n  marketplace surfaces are per-REPO files, not per-package, so a marketplace listing\n  some other product parses fine, yields nothing for this package, and reads as\n  `no-version` with nothing corrupt anywhere. `version` is likewise optional in an\n  upstream plugin manifest.\n- Honest residual: both fields are self-authored, and nothing checks that a declared\n  surface is one the sync command actually produces. Deriving that from the sync channel\n  was tried and withdrawn as unbuildable (the sync report names the plugin root as a directory, so two of the four surfaces never appear in it). The authoring repo's own D48 record of that is authoring-repo-internal and does not ship with this skill.\n\n## Artifact Rule\n\nThe durable release artifact filename is fixed:\n\n- `latest.md`\n\nDated release records should use `<repo-root>/charness-artifacts/release/YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>.md`.\n\n### Drafted release notes filename\n\nRelease notes drafted ahead of publish must carry `notes` as a whole\n`-`/`_`-delimited token in the filename, alongside the version:\n\n- `<date>-<tag>-notes.md`\n- `notes-<tag>.md` (the token may lead or trail)\n- `<date>-<tag>-public-notes.md` (dash-separated version tokens are recognised)\n\nThis is the shape the pre-publish refusal looks for when it checks whether notes\nwere drafted for the target tag and something else is being published. A draft\nthat omits the token — `v1.2.3.md`, `<date>-<version>-release.md` — is INVISIBLE\nto that arm, and publish will proceed with a generated body while the drafted\nnotes sit unshipped. That is the v2.11.0 escape, and the convention is the part\nof it a filename can fix.\n\nDeliberately narrow: the role word is matched as a whole token, not a substring,\nand no other word is recognised. A wider match once made a dated\n`<date>-<version>-release-record.md` read as drafted notes, and the refusal's\nremedy asks the operator to rename or delete the file — advice that must never\npoint at durable evidence.\n\n`release` should treat the packaging manifest as the canonical mutable version\nsource. Generated plugin manifests and marketplace files are derived surfaces\nand should be rewritten by the declared sync command, not edited by hand.\n\n`update_instructions` should name the canonical operator-facing refresh path for\nalready published installs. Keep them evergreen: describe how to install the\nlatest published release, not what changed in one release. Put release-specific\nbehavior changes, migration notes, rollback advice, and rationale in release\nnotes or the generated release artifact. Avoid host-internal compatibility detail\nunless operators truly need it to complete the update.\n\n`requested_review_commands` is optional and exists for release workflows where\nthe maintainer asks for a concrete review gate before publish. If any command\nfails, `check_requested_review_gate.py` blocks publish/tag instead of treating\nthe missing review surface as a caveat. The same helper scans release records\nfor configured unavailable-review phrases; those records need a fix or an\nexplicit review waiver phrase before release.\n\n`requested_review_policy` controls the empty-command posture. The default\n`warn-if-unconfigured` keeps legacy repos noisy when no requested-review command\nis wired. Repos that intentionally treat requested review as advisory declare\n`advisory-only`; the checker then records `configuration_status: advisory_only`\nwithout a warning. A configured command still blocks on failure under either\npolicy.\n\n`fresh_checkout_probes` is optional command data owned by the repo adapter.\nEach entry is a bash shell string executed from the temporary clone root. Use\nit for release evidence that can pass in a maintainer worktree but fail from a\nfresh checkout because of clone depth, generated artifact determinism, or other\ncheckout-shape assumptions.\n\n`check_fresh_checkout_probes.py` reports `passed`/`blocked` (exit 0/1) only when\nit actually ran the probes, which it does only under `--run-probes`. Declared but\nnot run is `not_established` at exit 3 (`run-quality.sh`'s `UNESTABLISHED_EXIT`),\ncarrying no `probe_results` key, because a listing is not a pass. A repo that\ndeclares no probes is `not_configured` at exit 0 — a genuine opt-out that never\nrefuses. `current_release.py` embeds the listing, so its fresh-checkout block is\nalways `not_established` or `not_configured`, never a probe verdict.\n\n`publish_release.py --execute` runs declared probes in a temporary\nshallow fresh clone after the release commit is created and before tag push or\nrelease creation, records passing status in the release artifact, and reruns\nthe probes against that amended release commit. A failing probe blocks publish.\n\nWhen `product_surfaces` contains both `installable_cli` and `bundled_skill`,\nrelease runs the CLI plus bundled-skill disclosure gate only for matching CLI,\nskill, plugin, package, or install-surface changes. Use\n`cli_skill_surface_probe_commands` to point at binary-owned help, registry,\ncatalog, example, version, install-smoke, doctor, or readiness probes. Keep\nthese probes local and deterministic; latest-release, network, or upstream\nfreshness checks belong in the release-specific proof that is intentionally\nchecking freshness. Use\n`cli_skill_surface_skill_paths` when the shipped skill does not live under\n`skills/public/*` or `skills/support/*`.\n\n`release_backend` mirrors the `issue_backend` shape — with ONE measured exception, in the\nposition that matters most for a template author: a `release_backend` command template\n**includes the binary as its first element**, and `backend_command` never reads\n`release_backend.binary`. (`issue_backend` templates exclude it and the issue backend prepends\n`issue_backend.binary`.) That difference is why the two rules are not one function, and it is\nexecuted rather than argued in\n`<authoring-repo>/tests/quality_gates/test_release_backend_agrees_with_the_owner.py`.\n\nA template part containing a literal brace that is not a placeholder (a JSON payload, say)\nis rendered through `str.format`, so double the braces (`{{\"q\":1}}`) or it fails fast.\n\nOtherwise `release_backend` mirrors `issue_backend` so release auth probes,\nrelease-existence checks, and release-create calls can route through the\nadapter-resolved CLI binary. Default is `{id: gh, binary: gh, commands: null}`,\nwhich keeps the existing `gh release ...` shape. Hosts that resolve releases\nthrough a different binary supply `commands` templates for `auth_check`,\n`release_view` (uses `{tag}` substitution), `release_create` (uses `{tag}` and\n`{title}` substitution), and optionally `release_view_body` (uses `{tag}`).\nWithout commands, a non-`gh` backend errors at runtime instead of falling back\nto `gh`.\n\n`release_view_body` reads the PUBLISHED release body back so the post-create\nnotes audit can inspect it — the pre-publish audit only runs when a notes FILE\nis supplied, so `--generate-notes` otherwise publishes a body nothing has seen.\nIt is the one op a backend may omit safely: it runs after the release exists,\nits result is advisory, and a missing template is recorded as\n`published_notes_audit: not-configured` rather than raising. Declaring it means\nthe audit actually inspects your published notes.\n\nThe placeholder set is enforced per op at runtime by\n`publish_release_helpers.backend_command`: `release_view` accepts `{tag}`,\n`release_create` accepts `{tag}` and `{title}`, and `auth_check` accepts no\nplaceholders. An adapter template using an unknown placeholder fails fast with\nthe offending placeholder named, matching the issue-backend close-with-comment\npattern (`issue_close._resolve_op` per-op allowlist) so both backend surfaces\nshare the same hardening shape. Adding a new placeholder requires updating\n`OP_PLACEHOLDERS` in `publish_release_helpers.py` plus the call site and a\nregression test.\n",

return {**payload, \"liveness\": task_run_runtime.runner_liveness(payload)}\n\n\ndef test_invalid_task_run_inputs_are_typed_and_actionable(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    repo = _repo(tmp_path)\n\n task_run_runtime.resolve_executor_executable(\"definitely-missing-muse\", executor=\"muse\")\n    with pytest.raises(task_run.TaskRunError, match=\"--lane must be a non-empty id\"):\n      raise KeyboardInterrupt\n\n    monkeypatch.setattr(task_run_execution, \"run_monitored_phase\", interrupting_phase)\n    monkeypatch.setattr(\n        task_run_execution.os,\n stderr_log=tmp_path / \"stderr.log\",\n        timeout_seconds=1,\n    )\n\n    assert result[\"exec_error\"] == \"cannot execute codex\"\n\n\ndef test_codex_arguments_fix_luna_sandbox_and_effort(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    git_common_dir = tmp_path / \".git\"\n    git_common_dir.mkdir()\n    assert task_run.build_codex_args(\n     \"gpt-5.6-luna\",\n        \"-c\",\n        \"model_reasoning_effort=xhigh\",\n    ]\n\n\ndef test_codex_effort_is_limited_to_orchestrator_presets() -> None:\n    with pytest.raises(task_run.TaskRunError, match=\"medium, xhigh, max\"):\n        task_run.build_codex_args(effort=\"high\")\n\n\ndef test_task_run_lane_shorthand_owns_safe_defaults_and_external_target(\n    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch\n) -> None:\n    repo = _repo(tmp_path)\n    executable = _codex(tmp_path, \"exit 0\")\n    monkeypatch.setenv(\"TMPDIR\", str(tmp_path / \"runtime-base\"))\n\n    payload = task_run.run_task(\n        repo,\n        lane=\"short-lane\",\n        scopes=[\"module.py\"],\n        prompt=\"update the module\",\n        codex=str(executable),\n        effort=\"xhigh\",\n        dry_run=True,\n    )\n\n    assert payload[\"status\"] == \"pass\"\n    assert payload[\"lane\"] == \"short-lane\"\n    assert payload[\"task_id\"] == \"short-lane\"\n task_run._checkout_own_dir({\"_checkout\": carrier})\n\n\ndef test_task_run_assigns_distinct_lane_runtime_roots_and_releases_finished_lanes(\n    tmp_path: Path,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Each lane gets `result.json`, because a finished\n    commit-only lane's `runtime/` and worktree are released at completion (#787):\n    the lane branch carries the whole candidate, so the worktree adds nothing.\n    \"\"\"\n    repo = _repo(tmp_path)\n    executable = _codex(\n        tmp_path,\n        'mkdir -p \"$CHARNESS_RUNTIME_ROOT\"\\n'\n        'record=\"$(dirname \"$CHARNESS_RUNTIME_ROOT\")\"\\n'\n \"$XDG_CACHE_HOME\" > \"$record/observed-paths\"\\n'\n        \"printf 'VALUE = 2\\\\n' > module.py\\n\"\n        \"git add -- module.py\\n\"\n        \"git -c user.email=test@example.com -c user.name=test commit -m 'update module'\",\n    )\n\n    payloads = [\n        task_run.run_task(\n            repo,\n            target_path=tmp_path / f\"lane-{task_id}\",\n            branch=f\"lane/{task_id}\",\n            base=\"HEAD\",\n            scopes=[\"module.py\"],\n            prompt=\"update the module\",\n            codex=str(executable),\n            effort=\"medium\",\n            task_id=task_id,\n        )\n        for task_id in (\"runtime-one\", \"runtime-two\")\n    ]\n\n    execution_roots = [Path(payload[\"execution_runtime_root\"]) for payload in payloads]\n    assert len({root.resolve() for root in execution_roots}) == 2\n    for payload, execution_root in zip(payloads, execution_roots):\n        assert payload[\"status\"] == \"completed\", payload\n        result_dir = Path(payload[\"result_path\"]).parent\n        assert execution_root == result_dir / \"runtime\"\n        assert (result_dir / \"observed-root\").read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\").strip() == str(\n            execution_root\n        )\n        observed_paths = (result_dir / \"observed-paths\").read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\").splitlines()\n        assert all(Path(path).is_relative_to(execution_root) for path in observed_paths)\n        assert task_run.task_status(repo, payload[\"task_id\"]) == _with_liveness(payload)\n\n        retention = payload[\"retention\"]\n        assert retention[\"worktree\"] == \"removed\"\n        assert retention[\"runtime\"] == \"removed\"\n        assert retention[\"carrier\"] == f\"{payload['target_branch']}@{payload['target_sha']}\"\n        assert payload[\"keep_worktree\"] is False\n        assert f\"branch {payload['target_branch']} at {payload['target_sha']}\" in payload[\"next_step\"]\n        assert not execution_root.exists()\n        assert not Path(payload[\"worktree_path\"]).exists()\n        assert Path(payload[\"result_path\"]).is_file()\n        assert Path(payload[\"logs\"][\"stdout\"]).is_file()\n        assert Path(payload[\"logs\"][\"stderr\"]).is_file()\n        # The candidate
